## Document 003: 10月FOMC会议点评：再探沃什底色

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月FOMC会议点评：再探沃什底色.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

事项：
美东时间 10 月 28日，美联储宣布将联邦基金利率目标区间维持在 3.75%—4.00%不变。
评论：
10月 FOMC如期暂停加息，会议基调中性偏鹰。美东时间 10月 28 日，美联储以 10比 2 将联邦基金利率目
标区间维持在 3.75%-4.00%，库克和哈马克主张加息 25BP。声明将经济扩张速度由“稳健”（solid）上
调为“强劲”（strong），同时强调积累更多信息有助于确定适当政策立场。
议息会议后，市场对 12月加息概率定价并未改变，但对 2027年末政策利率的预期小幅下移，表明本次暂
停主要缓和了市场对后续累计收紧幅度的担忧，尚未改变年内再加息一次的主流判断。因此，市场对会议
作出了边际偏鸽的反应。短端美债受益、曲线趋陡，两年期美债收益率下行 6.2BP，10 年和 30 年期分别
上行 0.6BP、2.2BP。美元指数下跌约 0.24%，伦敦现货黄金上涨约 0.86%，标普 500 和纳斯达克分别上涨
约 0.08%和 0.27%。我们认为，10月 FOMC是沃什政策哲学的试金石，暂停逻辑与后续政策安排体现其对增

长和通胀的权衡。
 政策“试金石”：从暂停的理由，观察沃什的政策哲学
我们认为，10 月 FOMC 为观察沃什的政策哲学提供了一个窗口。当就业（非农偏弱）与通胀（通胀仍高）
指向不同方向时、再叠加中期选举前夕，他选择如何行动、又如何解释，更能体现其政策底色。
暂停行动、保持鹰派底色是联储当下可行性最高、代价最小的一条路。沃什虽然释放鹰派信号，但政策执
行仍带有偏鸽倾向。声明以“积累更多信息”（the accumulation of more information）解释暂停。但
高油价叠加去年四季度基数走低，预计 10月乃至 Q4 通胀仍将高位运行。因此，沃什的选择有两种解释：
①确实需要等待，但重点在于弱非农是否延续、AI 能够承受多少收紧，权衡的是进一步加息的代价；②底
色倾向暂停，“等待信息”只是保留政策余地的说法或接口，其中也不乏中期选举前求稳的考虑。前者说
明他仍顾及增长的代价，市场不必担心其不计代价地抗通胀；后者若获验证，则意味着其借经济理由维持
独立决策的表象，实际在一定程度上被政治裹挟，底色是“鹰嘴鸽”。

现有表态更支持第一种解释。沃什希望先确认利率对 AI 投资的约束效果，避免追加不必要的紧缩。在新
闻发布会上，他质疑 AI 投资“无惧加息”的说法，并将部分信用利差走阔、投资者更加挑剔视为政策起
效的迹象。我们判断，后续观察政策应当考虑新变量——AI产业链的利率敏感性。当前科技巨头现金流可
能面临压力，依赖外部融资的数据中心和基础设施企业仍会承压。若适度收紧便能约束 AI 投资，联储就
不必通过更大幅度加息。若适度加息后，AI 产业链仍健康且价格压力仍不降温，继续等待的必要性会下降。

### Page 2

 政策安排：收紧有所节制，沟通框架调整
本次会议还明确了资产负债表调整与议息会议安排的变化，为判断后续货币政策提供了新线索。
一是资产负债表调整短期内不会出现，近期收紧仍以利率工具为主。沃什表示，工作组建议预计于 2027
年一季度初提交 FOMC，相关调整不太会对本轮政策周期产生实质影响。我们认为，这降低了近期缩表
（quantitative tightening, QT）推进的预期。在长端利率高企、企业融资成本已经上升的背景下，暂
不叠加缩表，有助于控制金融压力，也为评估前期加息效果保留空间。
二是减少例行利率决策次数与淡化前瞻指引相互配合。每年八次会议总数不变，但 2027 年拟有一次、2028
年拟有两次转为战略讨论，预计不进行利率投票、不发布政策声明。结合沃什对前瞻指引的保留态度，我
们认为，这一调整有助于减少市场围绕例行决议反复博弈，为政策效果的观察留出更长窗口，缓和弱化指
引带来的预期扰动。后续政策路径的判断，将更加依赖经济数据及联储决策标准的一致性。
 往后看：加息仍有空间，节奏取决于政策传导

我们维持 12月加息 25BP、2027年另加 1-2 次的基准判断。本次暂停并未改变联储将通胀带回 2%的目标，
重点在于评估进一步收紧的必要性。我们认为，后续加息幅度取决于两股力量。能源涨价是否向核心分项
扩散，以及融资成本上升能否约束 AI投资与其他需求。
对市场而言，政策节奏缓和有利于短端和估值修复，长端持续回落仍需通胀改善配合。短端美债对加息路
径下修的反应更直接，因此直接受益。长端则同时受到增长、通胀补偿与债券供需的影响，暂停加息本身
不足以推动收益率持续下行。
后续判断政策路径，应同时比较通胀持续与收紧过度两侧的代价，避免由单月数据线性外推。价格端看核
心动能与涨价广度是否同步变化，传导端看融资收紧是否真正影响 AI 投资，就业端看短期波动是否演变
为持续走弱。三组证据共同决定联储继续加息的必要性与承受空间，也是检验沃什政策取舍的依据。

## Document 061: 2026年7月投資月報

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年7月投資月報.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資月報)
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 12

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
AI 估值疑慮引發波動，惟股市仍有多頭可期
2026 年 7 月
全球主要股市 6 月 （截至24 日） 報酬跌多漲少，與5 月新興亞洲及 AI 半導體題材主導下的多頭
格局形成鮮明對比，主要因為市場同時承受美伊衝突升溫、 Fed 新主席華許上任後鷹派轉向，以及
AI 類股估值偏高引發的修正壓力，風險資產動能熄火。以美元計價6 月表現觀察，MSCI 中國與拉丁
美洲指數分別下跌 5.9%及 4.5%，表現最差。中國主因房市持續疲弱與內需復甦不均，並受其對中東
原油進口的高度依賴拖累 ， 拉丁美洲則受前波累積漲幅較大後的獲利了結與美元期間走強雙重壓抑。

美股因 AI 估值疑慮再起而拉回，拖累MSCI 新興市場與新興亞洲分別下跌 1.3%。惟有 MSCI 印度逆
勢上漲 1.9%，主要受惠 6 月下旬國際油價回落帶動。整體而言，6 月股市回檔較像是高基期後的短
線修正，並未改變今年資金偏好新興亞洲、日本與科技成長市場的方向。
6 月（截至 24 日）全球債市呈現「利率類承壓、信用類抗跌」的結構性分歧。全球主權債券單
月下跌 1.03%，跌幅居首，主因華許於 6 月會議釋出鷹派訊號，18 位官員中有 9 位於點陣圖計入今
年內至少一次升息，使美國 10 年期公債殖利率一度升至 4.5%，存續期間較長之利率類債券遭逢重
估。全球投資級公司債下跌 0.67%，跌幅次之，亦受存續期間拖累。新興當地債小跌 0.12%，反映
因美元指數回升使來自匯率的貢獻由正轉負。信用類債券則表現相對 較佳，新興美元債券單月上漲
0.53%、亞洲高收益債上漲 0.49%領先，主要受惠信用利差續處歷史低檔、企業基本面強健且違約率
仍偏低、到期牆延後且供給以再融資為主。

近期股市雖因 Fed 升息疑慮及 AI 類股估值過高而修正，但美光財報及蘋果商品漲價均印證 AI
產品供需緊俏且企業資本支出依然強勁 。 市場近期以類股輪動方式進行修正 ， 顯示投資人對於整體股
市信心仍在。在投資策略上，短期不宜追逐高估值個股，但可於震盪時分批布局 AI 基礎建設、半導
體供應鏈及台日股市。
債市方面，當前美債提供高於過往均值的高殖利率，將可相當程度抵銷殖利率續揚的風險。然因
投資人對 Fed 後續貨幣政策存有疑慮，或導致殖利率曲線短端受升息預期而有較大波動；長端又有
期限溢酬偏高、政府財政惡化及公債供給增加等風險。因此，建議債券佈局可聚焦 3 至 10 年期美國
公債及投等債，不僅能提供相對具吸引力的息收，若未來通膨預期放緩，在資本利得亦有表現機會。

身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
美國股市：企業盈餘亮點集中 AI，股市評價尚屬合理，經濟成長趨緩但韌性猶存
美股第一季財報顯示，標普 500 成分股獲利有八成企業超越市場預期，整體每股盈餘同比增長
26.2%，創下 2021 年以來新高，企業盈餘上修動能強勁。隨運算需求從大模型訓練轉向推論，市場
資金快速轉向記憶體、光通訊、ASIC、CPU 與通用型伺服器供應鏈，引領板塊快速輪動。接下來第
二季 AI 產業仍將帶動資訊業獲利高增長，而能源、原物料產業第二季財報仍有不錯表現，但消費、
金融、醫療等，甚至通訊服務產業都有成長放緩跡象。
美國關稅談判正轉向以「301 法案」推進，目前針對「強迫勞動貨品進口禁令」，美國貿易代表

署（USTR） 已建議對全球60 個經濟體加徵 10%與 12.5%的額外關稅 。 預料下半年陸續將有更多301
調查結果公布，這將為川普關稅談判提供法源依據，迫使各國供應鏈加速赴美設廠，為美國製造業注
入投資動能，故對美國整體經濟表現無須看淡。
伴隨企業獲利預期上修 ， 標普500 指數前瞻本益比目前仍在 21.45 倍水準 ， 且通訊服務僅在20.9
倍、資訊科技僅在 24.8 倍，估值水平依舊合理。當前市場質疑重點在於AI 產業吸引大量資金投入資
本支出，故縮減企業發放現金股利與股票回購的能力，然而考量下半年仍有期中選舉行情可期，且
AI 產業變革快速，新題材層出不窮，操作上維持分批布局的建議。
美國經濟在 2026 年表現顯示成長趨緩但韌性猶存，2026 年第一季 GDP 成長率為 2.6%，表現
仍優於歐元區、日本等其他市場。失業率維持在低檔，5 月非農新增就業仍有 17.2 萬人，失業率持
平於 4.3%。5 月 CPI 年增率升至 4.3%， 核心CPI 年增率加速到 2.9%， 能源價格仍為推升通膨主因；

同月份 PPI 與核心 PPI 分別來到 6.5%與 4.9%， 顯示上游成本壓力正持續向下游轉嫁。勞動市場結構
性分歧，金融就業人數下滑，然而資料中心、半導體與電力等基礎建設投資，拉動非住宅營建業與製
造業僱傭需求，支撐整體就業數據，消費端方面，5 月美國零售銷售月增率回升至 0.9%，其中加油
站銷售與無店鋪零售商銷售(電商)表現強勁，前月走弱的汽車及零部件銷售逆勢反彈，顯示在K 型經
濟支撐下，高收入群體與特定消費仍表現穩健。
預料新任主席華許在確立聯準會改革方案前不急於升息，但目前利率仍處歷史高位，對房市、汽
車和消費造成壓抑 。 觀察密西根大學消費者信心指數在第二季來到有紀錄以來最低 ， 且美國新屋開工
下降，房市持續低迷，美國經濟仍需消化高利率與通膨帶來的負面效應。

歐洲股市：歐洲央行態度轉鷹，靜待美伊停火與關稅明朗化
歐洲央行在 6 月會議升息 1 碼，並持續警告中東地緣政治風險可能為通膨前景增添不確定性，因
此將今年通膨預期上調至 3.0%（前值 2.6%） ， 預計要到明年下半年才有機會回到 2% 的政策目標 。
不過，與 2022 年通膨高峰時期不同，目前歐洲就業市場並未出現明顯過熱，薪資壓力也未持續升
溫，因此我們認為本次升息後，歐洲央行更可能轉向觀望，利率期貨則顯示，市場仍預期年底前有再
升息 1 碼的可能性。
歐洲央行本次會議將邊際貸款利率、主要再融資利率及存款機制利率上調 1 碼至 2.65% 、

2.4% 與 2.25% ，會議中所有委員均同意升息。勞動力需求進一步降溫，薪資成長預計將在一年內
放緩，目前沒有看到與工資相關的二次效應，本次會議未討論中性利率和中性區間，如果不升息，通
膨因此失控 ， 重新控制將更加困難 。GDP 成長預估來看 ，2026 年爲 0.8%，2027 年爲 1.2%，2028
年爲 1.5% 。通膨方面，2026 年爲 3%，2027 年爲 2.3%，2028 年爲 2.0% 。核心通膨來看，
2026 年爲 2.5%，2027 年爲 2.5%，2028 年爲 2.2% 。
歐洲經濟成長動能放緩，但製造業維持擴張態勢，雖然歐洲央行將 2026、2027 年經濟成長率預
估值下調，自 0.9%、1.3%調整至 0.8%、1.2%。然 6 月歐元區綜合 PMI 自 48.5 回升至 49.5，顯示
景氣萎縮幅度正逐步收窄，製造業活動仍維持在擴張區間，同月服務業 PMI 雖僅 48.9，但也已釋出
跌勢趨緩訊號。
ECB 升息 1 碼對抗通膨風險，後續升息路徑分歧，受美伊衝突引發的油價影響，歐洲通膨壓力再

度上升，5 月歐元區 CPI 年增率已達 3.2%，為防範通膨失控，歐洲央行在 6 月利率決策會議將存款
利率從 2.0%上調至 2.25%。目前市場對歐洲央行後續的升息路徑看法顯著分歧，鷹派陣營認為 6 月
升息 1 碼後、第三季會再升 1 碼，並維持利率水平至 2027 年底；鴿派觀點認為隨油價回跌，短期內
再升息必要性降低，故後續仍將觀察各國通膨轉嫁能力。
待美伊戰事落幕、美歐關稅底定，歐股可望啟動估值修復，展望後市，若美伊停火協議落實、引
領國際油價回吐先前漲幅，將成為扭轉歐股當前弱勢、並吸引國際資金回流的催化劑。此外，美國改
採以 301 條款課徵關稅，對歐洲出口實質影響仍須觀察，惟預料美歐盟友關係穩固，終將透過談判
達成雙邊協議。目前泛歐 600 本益比以 2026 年獲利僅 15.6 倍。待短期不確定性因素陸續消退，市
場情緒可望改善，有利啟動估值修復行情。

日本市場：成長動能仰賴出口，政府補貼支撐景氣表現，股市屢創新高
GDP 動能放緩，受惠族群集中於AI，日本 2026 年第一季 GDP 年增率僅 0.4%，創下 2024 年走
出經濟衰退以來最疲弱表現，市場已將全年度 GDP 成長預估下調至 0.6%。然而 5 月出口年增來到
17%，創 2022 年 11 月以來最快增速，其中半導體相關出口年增逾六成、反映全球資料中心需求全
面爆發，推升記憶體晶片與電子零組件價格走揚，故受惠族群集中，下半年成長動能仍高度仰賴 AI
產業。
財政補貼壓低通膨 ， 油價回跌降低財政負擔，近期 ，首相高市早苗推行 「責任的積極財政」 政策，

透過實施電費、瓦斯補貼，並將全國汽油價格維持在每公升約170 日圓水平。未來隨補貼措施退場，
預期物價將出現階段性反彈。日本央行已於利率決策會議把本年度核心通膨預估上調至 2.8%。然而
在利率走高背景下，日本政府財政結構惡化，根據2026 財年政府預算規劃，國債利息支出將達總預
算的 10.65%，明顯高於前一財年的 9.13%。所幸美伊戰事有降溫跡象，預期日本政府將持續縮減刺
激方案規模。
日銀升息 1 碼，美日利差未收斂壓抑日圓表現，且日本央行於 6 月會議決定升息 1 碼至 1.0%，
主要考量國內物價上行風險加劇、薪資維持強勁成長動能，且經濟下行風險受控。市場普遍預期下次
升息落在 2026 年第四季。然而高市若為緩解財政壓力，可能干預日銀升息路徑。鑑於美日利差未出
現明顯收斂 ， 即使日本官方進場干預 ， 對日圓匯率的支撐效果恐怕相對有限 ， 日圓匯率預估於155-163
區間震盪。
日經指數近期屢創新高，6 月 22 日史上首度突破 72,000 點、連七個交易日刷新紀錄。上漲動能

來自日圓貶至 161 附近，顯著優化出口企業的盈餘表現，同時「實體 AI」投資計畫引爆半導體與 AI
類股行情 。 就2026 年以來 ， 領漲標的涵蓋東京威力科創 、 村田製作所 、 鎧俠 、 軟銀、 三菱UFJ 金融、
愛德萬測試、松下控股、三井住友金融、瑞穗金融、藤倉等。上漲族群主要為 AI 供應鏈以及受惠於
升息的金融族群，但即使過去一年日股飆升，目前以 2026 年獲利，本益比仍在 24 倍合理位置。
波克夏深化日本投資佈局，外資買盤同步加碼，波克夏對日本資產配置持續增加。自 2020 年對
伊藤忠、丸紅、三菱商事、三井物產、住友商事等五家各以略高於 5%持股起步，至 2026 年 5 月對
各家持股均已突破 10%， 顯示加碼日本仍是其核心策略 。 與此同時 ， 外資對日股買盤持續湧入 ，2026
年迄今(截至 6 月 20 日為止)外資買超日股的累計金額擴大至 10.2 兆日圓 ， 反映國際資本對日股後勢
依舊樂觀。

台灣股市：AI 需求支撐製造業 PMI 高檔擴張，暑假旺季與股市推升服務動能
當前臺灣經濟正處於生產擴張的黃金期。最新公布的總體經濟數據與半導體龍頭營收呈現全面共
振，印證全球 AI 資本支出（CapEx）並非流於故事題材，而是實質轉化為台廠的訂單與獲利。儘管
全球金融市場短期受到地緣政治干擾與美債殖利率波動影響 ， 引發台股在高檔震盪 ， 但主計總處大幅
上修 2026 年 GDP 預測至 9.64%，配合製造業 PMI 續擴張至 60.7%，顯示基本面強勁。我們認
為，台股高檔的回檔屬於健康的獲利了結與籌碼沉澱，長線多頭架構未變。建議採取拉回布局策略，

聚焦 AI 實體基礎設施與邊緣運算（Edge AI）雙主線。
國發會公布之 6 月製造業 PMI 報 60.7， 連續第 6 個月維持在 60.0%以上的擴張速度 ， 六大產業
中，僅食品暨紡織產業與交通工具回報緊縮。細項中，新增訂單與生產分別上揚至 60.4（前 59.6）
與 58.7（前 57.9） ，同時原物料存貨和客戶存貨分別回跌至 61.9（前 65.7）和 49.4（前 50.5），
顯示在 AI 伺服器、半導體與高階製造的強勁拉貨下，庫存水位仍屬可控。
5 月海關出口達 784.8 億美元（年增 51.7%） ，寫下連 31 紅紀錄。其中，資通訊產品出口年
增 75.2%，電子零組件出口創歷史新高。同時，5 月進口額創下 605.7 億美元歷史新高（年增
54.9%） ，核心物資多為半導體資本設備，依進口領先出口 1 至 2 季之歷史慣例，預示下半年出貨
動能將再創高峰。台積電 5 月合併營收達 4,169 億 7,500 萬元（年增 30.1%） 。先進製程與高階
封裝產能持續滿載，且透過定價調整順利維持毛利率在高檔，讓獲利能見度直接延伸至後續數年。

主計總處正式將 2026 全年經濟成長率（GDP）預測大幅上修 1.93 個百分點至 9.64%，創下
16 年來新高紀錄。第一季 GDP 初估更達 14.55%，創 48 年來單季新高。 總經結構分析顯示，
這波榮景是由「出口拉動投資、投資進而促進消費」的良性循環。全球主要雲端服務供應商（CSP）
調高資本支出，其新一代伺服器建置「必須向台灣採購」 。商品出口實質成長預估將帶動全年商品出
口總值達 8,945 億美元，其年增率創 50 年來最高水準，構成台灣 GDP 增長的關鍵引擎。
儘管台股指數在高檔震盪加劇，但在 9.64% 的 GDP 強勁基本面保護下，下檔支撐極度紮實。
隨著 COMPUTEX 後終端裝置陸續開出 ， 消費性電子將迎來升級週期 ， 可望讓下半年經濟加速升溫。
我們持續看多台灣股市。

中國股市：製造業需求回溫，然動能僅集中於高新技術產業與出口端
中國 6 月製造業需求回溫，動能集中新產業與出口端，製造業 PMI 為 50.3（前 50.0） ，較前
月回升，並連續四個月維持在擴張區間。主要動能來自新出口訂單回升至 50.1（前 48.6） ，以及新
訂單升至 51.2（前 49.9） ，皆較前月回升至擴張區間，顯示需求端及出口端表現亦有所回溫。生產
經營活動預期上升至 54.3（前 53.9） ，製造業前景保有一定韌性，細項觀察，6 月生產指數 51.4
（前 51.2） ，持續維持在擴張區間，本月在新訂單回升、庫存指數回落至 47.7（前 49.3）之下，新

訂單減庫存擴大至 3.5（前 0.6） 。 另外受到美伊衝突緩解影響 ， 主要原材料價格大幅回落至 54.2（前
60.5） ，外部壓力緩解。
產業方面，新舊動能延續上月分化格局。高技術製造業 PMI 升至 53.5（前 52.9） ，裝備製造業
升至 52.5（前 52.1） ，上升趨勢持續，相較之下，傳統高耗能產業 47.1（前 47.1） ，建築業商業活
動 49.0（前 48.8） ，仍維持在較弱格局。整體來看，美伊衝突緩解有效帶動中國製造廠商轉趨樂觀，
但多數仍集中在新產業及出口端，後續仍待觀察新產業能否有效帶動內需動能回升。
然而，內需數據尚未見回溫：中國第二季以來的消費、固定投資數據下滑，房地產未見起色。工
業生產年增長 4.5%，表現最為堅挺，主要受到政策性資本開支與科技硬需求的支撐。零售銷售5 月
單月年增率轉負， 即便有五一黃金周的短暫拉動，但節後服務消費迅速墜落顯示出居民消費依然保
守。固定資產投資年比下降 4.1% 。 這是過往作為壓艙石的投資首次跌破 4% 防線。民間固定資

產投資年比更劇烈下滑 7.1%。
整體來說，中國政府大規模刺激的可能性偏低，主要政策料是調降 LPR 或結構性定向降準。其
政策核心在於為中下游民營中小企業減輕債務壓力。現行溫和的企業設備更新與家電 「以舊換新」 政
策邊際效益遞減。為遏制消費滑入通縮螺旋，中央財政下半年將擴大赤字，直接補貼居民端，如發放
消費券、加大育兒養老津貼等等。中國總體經濟正處於重組期，缺乏向上突破的動能，但向下有國家
政策工具與新興硬科技的實質托底 。 房地產全面反轉或大眾消費復甦的機率偏低 。 雖政府政策扶植的
新科技產業仍有機會，但整體看法仍偏向保守。

印度股市：外部供給衝擊與內部基本面的對抗，看法中性
全球供應鏈受到中東戰事的實質干擾 ， 印度的前瞻景氣指標卻給市場帶來了巨大的驚喜 。 最新
公布的 2026 年 5 月 HSBC 印度綜合採購經理人指數由 4 月的 58.2 逆勢回升至 59.3，創下近
6 個月來的最高擴張紀錄。其中，服務業 PMI 由 58.8 顯著彈升至 59.8，主因是國內消費模式在
中東局勢干擾下成功「外轉內」 ，內需服務動能十分強勁；製造業 PMI 5 月最終值亦來到 55.0，高
於 4 月的 54.7。 雖然新出口訂單因中東衝突擴大而減速 ， 但國內基礎建設與製造業的 「預防性囤貨」

需求，成功支撐製造業維持在歷史平均水準之上。
然而，內需並無法完全沖刷外部供給側帶來的窒息感。印度高達 85% 的原油依賴進口，中東
戰事導致國際油價攀升至每桶近 120 美元，對印度財政與物價造成極大考驗。最新數據顯示，5 月
份消費者物價指數（CPI）年增率由 4 月的 3.5% 抬頭至 3.9%。這主要是受到汽車燃油價格凍結
政策結束的影響 。此外 ，比原油更長遠的隱憂來自於氣候 。 氣象機構預警有高機率出現乾旱 。 歷史上，
降雨不足與聖嬰現象會嚴重抑制糧食與作物產量 ， 豆類、 油籽等作物的受災風險顯著高於有灌溉保護
的水稻和小麥。彭博與野村等機構一致預警，這項氣候乾旱威脅將在 10 月農作物收成期引爆新一
輪食品通膨，預估將食品與飲料通膨推向 6.0%，並使下半年整體 CPI 衝破央行的容忍上限。
面對經常帳赤字擴大、國際收支（BOP）惡化與盧比貶值壓力，印度當局並未坐以待斃。首先
在貨幣政策方面，印度央行（RBI）於 6 月 5 日一致決議維持政策回購利率在 5.25% 不變，立場

維持「中性」 。央行認為盲目升息防禦匯率不僅無效，反而可能將能源衝擊轉化為內需重傷，因此決
策者選擇保留政策空間以依賴數據靈活決策。不過，經濟學家多數預期，隨著年底通膨風險逐漸壓過
成長擔憂，央行這項「按兵不動」的政策不會持續太久。 其次在財政工具方面，為保障佔人口半數
的農業人口並穩定農村經濟，政府宣布 2026/27 財年肥料補貼預算大增 20%，並階段性減免汽柴
油，支撐內需經濟。
印度市場目前正呈現明顯的「基本面強韌」與「評價面修正」的矛盾訊號。不可否認，5 月高
企的 PMI 與 1Q GDP 的暴衝證實其內部需求並未崩潰，而政府靈活的黃金關稅政策亦成功控制了
經常帳的惡化 。 然而 ， 中東戰事帶來的原油窒息感與下半年季風降雨不足 ， 是極難迴避的供給側利空 。
這兩項毒素預計將在第四季將通膨推向 6% 以上，進而逼迫央行轉為年底升息，更高的借貸成本屆
時將進一步抑制工業與內需需求，也讓股市有評價修正的壓力。因此，我們建議現階段應採取較為防
守的中長線投資策略，以在估值向下修正的震盪期中，鎖定長期結構性獲利。

拉美市場：巴西選舉風險與通膨預期升溫，後市應保守看待
6 月以來拉美市場主軸已由單純降息利多，轉為政治風險、通膨預期與央行動態三方拉扯。巴西
總統大選民調顯示魯拉領先幅度擴大，疊加巴西通膨預期與 Selic 利率路徑連續上修，使市場開始重
新評估 2026 年政策與財政不確定性，也使整體拉美股市表現持續承壓。
選舉方面，最新公布的 Genial/Quaest 民調指出，魯拉對波索納洛的領先幅度再度擴大，第一輪
由前次 39%對 33%，轉為本次 39%對 29%，魯拉領先差距升至 10 個百分點；第二輪亦由前次魯拉
42%對波索納洛 41%，轉為本次 44%對 38%，領先 6 個百分點。由於魯拉為較不受資本市場所青睞

的候選人，其當選可能性上升，預期將不利於巴西股市表現。
另一方面 ， 通膨預期惡化也使巴西降息交易持續降溫 。截至 6 月 15 日的巴西央行市場調查指出，
2026 年巴西通膨預期由 5.11%上修 19 個基點至 5.30%，持續高於 4.50%通膨目標區間上緣。2027
至 2029 年通膨預期亦全數高於 3.0%目標，顯示市場對通膨回到央行目標區間信心不足。
受此影響，市場對巴西政策利率路徑也持續轉鷹，2026、2027、2028 年底 Selic 利率預期分別
升至 13.75%、12.00%、10.25%，較前次的 13.50%、11.50%、10.00%再度上修。財政面同樣削弱
政策可信度，2026 至 2028 年基本財政餘額預期仍為 GDP 的-0.50%、-0.40%、-0.24%，名目財政
赤字 2026 至 2029 年亦維持超過 GDP 的 7%，顯示巴西財政赤字問題持續惡化。
貨幣政策方面，巴西央行 6 月 17 日全票決議降息 25 個基點，將 Selic 利率降至 14.25%，表面

上延續降息循環 ， 但其聲明與前瞻指引缺乏清晰度 ， 使市場更難判斷央行是否會對通膨預期重新升溫
採取足夠強硬態度。巴西央行通膨預測亦同步上修，2026 年底整體通膨預測由 4.6%升至 5.2%，自
由價格通膨預測在 2026 年第四季由 4.5%升至 5.3%，在通膨壓力持續升高的狀況下，巴西短期內進
一步降息空間已相當有限。
巴西以外拉美央行亦呈現類似政策兩難，智利央行 6 月 16 日宣布維持政策利率 4.5%不變 ， 即使
其 2026 年 GDP 成長預測降至 1%至 1.75%，但仍因油價與地緣風險推升短期通膨而選擇觀望。
展望後市，拉美市場在巴西大選風險升溫、通膨預期持續攀升的背景下，後市股市上行空間料將
受限，尤其全球股市近期受到 AI 題材帶動，拉美市場在缺乏成長敘事下，表現料將難以跟上全球指
數表現，故我們認為目前對拉美市場應保守看待。

科技類股：Token 經濟學與邊緣 AI 外溢效應，帶動科技股進一步走揚
當前全球科技股正處於由 AI 算力基礎設施引導的「結構性重新定價」新起點。首先半導體產值
將正式跨越破兆美元大關，市場對於「AI 泡沫破裂」的擔憂缺乏實質數據支持。超大型雲端服務商
（CSP） 具備強韌的資產負債表與 EBITDA 創造力，大舉發債並非盲目燒錢。「Token 經濟學」 打通
了算力消耗與商業變現的阻礙 ， 這些都是科技股的中長線利多。 雖然美國貨幣政策走向可能引發短線
估值震盪，但基本面依然偏多。
根據 IDC 最新報告，全球半導體市場規模正迎來歷史性的結構性跨越，2026 年全球半導體總營

收預估將達到 1.29 兆美元 ， 較 2025 年增 52.8%。 其中 ， 單是資料中心半導體營收就將達到 4,771
億美元，預計到 2030 年將達 8,432 億美元，獨佔市場半壁江山。2026 年全球記憶體營收暴增至
5,947 億美元，其中 DRAM 營收跳升至 4,186 億美元，年增率高達 177%。主因是 CSP 業者為
了搶奪 HBM 與高階 DDR 產能，願意支付極高溢價，推升整體板塊毛利率。
輝達執行長黃仁勳定調的 「Token 經濟學」 ，將技術敘事轉化為可量化的財務獲利模型，其核心
論點為 「Compute is revenue（運算就是收入） ，AI 工廠生產的實體商品就是 Token。 而產生Token
的數量就可以與資本開支互相匹配，進而由算力推估出資本開支的回報率。在 1GW 功耗限制下，
Blackwell 世代年營收約 300 億美元；進入次世代 Vera Rubin 晶片架構後，因運算密度拉升，相
同功耗下的年營收預估將暴增 5 倍至 1,500 億美元 ， 提供 CSP 業者持續加大資本支出的實質回報

率支持。
此外，隨著集中式雲端算力的建置跨過臨界點，AI 算力紅利正加速向終端裝置外溢。智慧型手機
與 PC 本地執行 AI 推論的需求已迎來全面爆發，Edge AI 正為半導體市場創造出全新的高價值需
求類別。邊緣 AI 擺脫了過往智慧終端僅比較出貨量的殺價競爭，轉向「高附加價值」的規格升級溢
價。長期而言，隨著 AI 代理（Agentic AI）在終端裝置的滲透率攀升，半導體將迎來新一波結構性
定價與穩健的增長動能。
短期來看，由於通膨數據具備黏性，市場正重新評估聯準會 （Fed） 貨幣政策再度升息的可能性。
此流動性緊縮預期直接推升了美債無風險利率 ，可能對高估值的科技權值股造成本益比階段性震盪修
正。然而，科技巨頭極其強韌的實質獲利能力足以消化估值衝擊。2026 年半導體跨越 1.29 兆美元
產值已成剛需，CSP 業者 EBITDA 預估增長 13%，配合黃仁勳 Token 經濟學的變現邏輯，獲利增
長將有效消化高本益比，建議可持續加碼科技類股。

能源類股：荷莫茲海峽運量逐步恢復，油價料將承壓
受到美伊戰爭持續朝收斂方向前進影響，市場重新評估波斯灣出口恢復後的供給回補壓力；同
時，近期中國原油需求與進口動能明顯轉弱，使油市主軸由短線斷供風險，轉向中期供給過剩與需求
破壞，受此影響，6 月油價出現大幅回落，相關能源類股表現也出現承壓。
高盛報告指出，自美伊 6 月 17 日達成臨時協議後，荷莫茲海峽與波斯灣出口流量快速回升，中
東局勢於 6 月 27 日油輪襲擊後再度升溫前，波斯灣石油流量七日均值已達每日 1,800 萬桶，約為戰
前水準近 80%。其中荷莫茲海峽流量在協議後前 10 日增加每日 700 萬桶，6 月 24 日單日估算流量

一度達每日 1,300 萬桶 ， 約為正常水準60%； 若依過去兩週平均每日增加100 萬桶的恢復速度推估，
波斯灣流量最快可在 7 月初回到戰前每日 2,300 萬桶水準。不過，6 月 27 日油輪襲擊後，多艘超大
型油輪傳出掉頭返航 ，6 月 27 日至 6 月 28 日前後經荷莫茲海峽出口量一度降至每日 500 萬桶以下 ，
不到正常水準 20%， 顯示中東石油出口恢復方向雖明確 ， 但短期內仍取決於船東對海峽安全的信心。
另一方面，需求端壓力也有望加速全球石油供需平衡。IEA（國際能源署）6 月月報估計，第二
季全球石油供應缺口為每日 310 萬桶 ， 低於高盛估計的每日 500 萬桶 ， 主因 IEA 對石油需求破壞
的估計更高，IEA 預估第二季需求破壞量為每日 550 萬桶，高於高盛估計的每日 490 萬桶。需求
端疲弱原因主要來自中國需求下滑。彭博分析指出，中國 5 月原油需求急劇下降，原油進口量降至
每日 680 萬桶，較去年同期下滑 40%，主因包含荷莫茲海峽關閉期間造成的採購干擾，以及經濟

放緩帶來的需求破壞。進一步看至 2027 年，IEA 預估在供給年增每日 790 萬桶，以及部分需求疲
弱延續下，全球每日將出現 500 萬桶供給過剩，高於高盛預估的每日 320 萬桶，顯示中期油市風
險已由短缺轉向過剩。
受到整體油市轉往供給過剩影響，高盛將 2026 年第四季布蘭特原油價格預測由每桶 90 美元下
修至 80 美元，2027 年平均價格由 80 美元下修至 75 美元；西德州原油方面，2026 年第四季均價
預估由 85 美元下修至 75 美元，2027 年則由 75 美元下修至 70 美元。摩根大通、巴克萊、摩根士
丹利、瑞銀與滙豐也紛紛下修今明兩年油價展望，預估 2027 年油價均價介於每桶 63 至 85 美元；
其中巴克萊預期第四季可能出現每日 300 萬桶供應過剩，滙豐則預期短期將出現小型供應過剩。
展望後市，能源類股仍需觀察美伊臨時備忘錄落實狀況、荷莫茲海峽運輸恢復及中東增產狀況是
否順暢，然而當前市場更為關注停戰帶來的風險溢價收斂，故中短期內油價表現料將較為疲軟，在此

狀況下 ， 能源類股表現料將難以勝過由 AI 題材帶動的全球指數表現 ，故對後市我們亦維持保守看法。

黃金類股：央行增持黃金趨勢未變，惟聯準會偏鷹不利金價表現
6 月以來黃金類股表現持續落後整體指數表現，主因受到美國聯準會態度偏鷹派影響，市場升息
預期上升，推升美元與美債殖利率走強，連帶提高黃金這類無孳息資產的機會成本，使金價與黃金類
股短線表現持續承壓。
6 月 FOMC 會議雖維持聯邦基準利率在 3.5%至 3.75%不變，但利率點陣圖中位數由 3 月時預估
2026 年底的 3.375%上修至 3.75%，同時 18 位提交預測委員中，有 9 位預期年內至少升息一次，8
位預期維持不變，僅 1 位預期降息 1 碼，導致會後市場升息預期明顯攀升。對黃金而言，升息預期

升溫將推升美元與美債殖利率 ， 並提高持有無息資產的機會成本 ， 尤其金價先前在美伊戰爭導致長天
期公債殖利率攀升的影響下，表現已相對疲弱，近期利率預期轉鷹更容易使短線獲利了結壓力升高。
另一方面，新任聯準會主席 Kevin Warsh 在本次會議中並未屈從政治面的降息壓力，反而重申
2%通膨目標並強調貨幣政策的獨立性，有助於強化市場對聯準會政策紀律的信心。此舉雖有助於提
升美國政策可信度，卻也將對黃金帶來不利影響；由於黃金的部分避險買盤，源自於市場對美國財政
負擔、政策獨立性及美元信用的疑慮，故一旦市場相信聯準會將維持堅定對抗通膨的立場，這類避險
需求便可能逐步減弱，也讓黃金類股失去美國政策性避險這項題材。
然而，儘管短線黃金逆風重重，但是在央行買盤方面，仍未見到全球央行對於增持黃金的看法有
所轉變。世界黃金協會所出版的《2026 年央行黃金儲備調查》顯示，89%受訪央行預期未來 12 個
月全球央行黃金儲備將增加 ， 雖低於2025 年的 95%， 但仍高於2024 年的 81%與 2023 年的 71%，

顯示多數國家央行對於持續增持黃金儲備仍具高度共識。就自身配置而言， 45%受訪央行預期未來
12 個月自身黃金儲備將增加，高於 2025 年的 43%與 2024 年的 29%，並創下 2019 年以來最高比
例，另有 54%預期維持不變，僅 1%預期下降，顯示各國央行黃金配置意願尚未出現明確轉向 態勢。
該項調查也指出，各國央行增持黃金的原因，主要仍集中在儲備多元化與風險分散。90%受訪央
行認為黃金在危機時期具有避險性 ，84%認為黃金具長期保值與通膨避險功能 ，83%認為黃金是有效
的投資組合分散工具，80%認為黃金屬於儲備多元化政策的一部分。由此可見，央行買金並非單純追
逐短線價格，而是與官方儲備安全性、幣別分散及危機應對需求有關。
展望後市，全球央行增持黃金的趨勢仍未改變，然而短線上，在降息利多消失、美元及美債殖利
率偏向上行，且聯準會獨立性疑慮降溫的背景下，金價表現料將受到壓抑，預期後市黃金類股表現將
於區間內震盪整理，目前對黃金類股後市中性看待。

### Page 12

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：市場觀望華許首秀，美債利率料陷整理走勢
6 月中的 FOMC 會議以 12–0 一致決議維持聯邦資金利率目標區間於 3.50%–3.75%， 並重申維
持充足準備金政策。然而，投資人所真正關注的內容在於會後所公布的經濟預測摘要(SEP)。在 6 月
點陣圖中，九位官員預計今年至少會升息 1 碼，其中六位預計至少升息 2 次。另有九位官員預計利
率維持不變或會降息 。2026 年底聯邦基金利率中位數由 3 月的 3.4% 上修到 3.8%，2027 年由 3.1%
上修到 3.6%；同時，2026 年 PCE 通膨預估由 2.7% 大幅上修至 3.6%，核心 PCE 由 2.7% 上修

至 3.3%。這代表 Fed 不只承認通膨反彈，也認為利率路徑需要重新往上定價。彭博對於 Fed 主席
開場談話的 NLP 情緒模型亦反映了鷹派傾向的變化。
華許在此次的會後記者會中雖重申聯準會對物價穩定與最大化就業雙重使命的承諾 ， 但針對已持
續五年多高於 2%目標的通膨問題，他強調委員會將毫不含糊地致力於恢復物價穩定。同時Fed 也將
維持銀行系統的充足準備金政策。他也指出，儘管面臨不確定性，目前經濟活動穩步擴張，生產力和
資本投資強勁，且就業市場保持穩定；本次會議發布了更簡短直接的政策聲明，取消了華許認為不合
時宜的 「前瞻性指引」 ，使得此次會後聲明用字大幅精簡 （由4/29 會議時的 341 字減至 130 字） ，
Warsh 本人也選擇不參與提交經濟預測摘要（SEP）。作為領導層上任後的重要革新，他也宣布在未
來兩週內正式成立五個獨立小組以分別對「溝通」、「資產負債表」、「數據來源」、「生產力／就
業」與「通膨框架」等議題進行改革，旨在打造一個適應時代需求且專注於未來的聯準會。

美國總體經濟環境現處於「經濟具韌性但通膨僵固」的微妙平衡。即便 FOMC 6 月會議立場偏
向鷹派，但仍約有半數委員認為年底前利率將維持現行水準。彭博指出，倘美伊停火協議得以維持，
通膨或於未來數月回落，部分 6 月預期升息的委員 9 月可望轉為按兵不動。元大投顧認為「年底前
維持利率不變仍是大概率事件」 ，因近期震盪部分是因市場「尚未適應 Warsh 新溝通風格」所產生
的過度反應。此外，高盛在 FOMC 會後發表的最新報告也指出即便  Fed「升息的可能性雖增加，
但仍非其基本預期」。
我們認為，當前美債已充分反映通膨上行風險，並提供高於過往均值的高殖利率，將可相當程度
抵銷殖利率進一步走高之風險。然因投資人對 Fed 的後續貨幣政策存有疑慮，或導致殖利率曲線短
端受升息預期變化而有較大波動；長端又有期限溢酬偏高、政府財政惡化及公債供給增加等風險，故
存續期過長亦非明智之舉。因此，建議債券佈局可聚焦 3 至 10 年期美國公債及投等債，不僅能提供
相對具吸引力的息收，若未來通膨預期隨中東緊張情勢放緩，在資本利得方面亦可望有所表現。

➢ 3Q26 毛利率回升，獲利大致符合預期 
3Q26 營收 253.34 億元(QoQ+9.6%)，成長來源中邏輯(非記憶體)與記
憶體各約一半；記憶體增加約 11 億元，主要來自 DRAM；YoY 成長中
NAND Flash 增加超過 20 億元 ，3Q26 毛利率 24%， 較前季增加2.2ppts
主要來自於產能利用率高檔以及價格調升，營業外利益 0.85 億元，稅後
獲利 26.12 億元，QoQ+17.7%，YoY+69.9%，EPS 3.44 元略低於我們
預估的 3.50 元。其中 3Q26 封裝、測試產能利用率皆超越 90%，後續
將藉由淘汰老舊機台維持在接近滿載水準， 4Q26 營收及毛利率持續改
善，3Q26 毛利率提升主要來自於整體產品毛利率結構改善(產品組合影
響不大)。 
➢ 4Q26 受惠於漲價趨勢，2026 年營運大幅改善 
以目前市況來看，MU 仍持續擴大 DRAM 委外訂單(Mobile、graphics 
DRAM、LPDDR5)，NAND 景氣回溫需求明顯，國票預估力成 4Q26
營收 264.32 億元(QoQ＋4.33%)，毛利率因價格調整上升到 24.2%，較
前一季增加 0.2ppts，Logic 產品也轉往高毛利為主 ， 淨利為32.68 億元 ，
QoQ＋25.1%，YoY+75.4%，EPS 4.31 元，在 DRAM/Flash 景氣仍處
在高檔的狀況下，子公司超豐也往高附加價值的 FCBGA 為主，Tera 
Probe 以高階測試為主，高階記憶體/LTA 比重持續上揚，也將帶動力成
營收及毛利率進一步放大，投顧預估力成 2026 年營收將達 961.96 億
元，EPS 13.10 元。 
➢ 2027 年展望持續樂觀 
AI Chip、OE 產品/CPO/TSV interconnection DRAM 目前驗證進度順
利，量產計畫維持不變，目標成為全球第一家量產 FOPLP AI 應用的公
司、與Broadcom 共同開發及生產用於先進封裝基板上的加成式細線寬
重佈層封裝技術順利，我們預估力成在整體 FOPLP/記憶體市況良好的
狀況下 ，2027 年營收為 1,139.7 億元(YoY+18.48%)，EPS 可以達到 17.97
元優異水準。 
➢ 維持買進評等，目標價 306 元

維持正向看法 ， 過去本益比區間位於6X~20X， 目標價306 元(2027F PE 
17X)。

個股報告

3Q26 毛利率回升，獲利大致符合預期 
3Q26 營收 253.34 億元(QoQ+9.6%)， 成長來源中邏輯(非記憶體)與記憶體各約一半 ；
記憶體增加約 11 億元，主要來自 DRAM；YoY 成長中 NAND Flash 增加超過 20 億
元，3Q26 毛利率 24%，較前季增加 2.2ppts 主要來自於產能利用率高檔以及價格調
升，營業外利益 0.85 億元，稅後獲利 26.12 億元，QoQ+17.7%，YoY+69.9%，EPS 
3.44 元略低於我們預估的 3.50 元。其中 3Q26 封裝、測試產能利用率皆超越 90%，
後續將藉由淘汰老舊機台維持在接近滿載的水準 ，4Q26 營收及毛利率持續改善 ，3Q26
毛利率提升主要來自於整體產品毛利率結構改善(產品組合影響不大)。營收結構：封裝 
67%、 測試24%(約 60.8 億元)、SIP 與模組 9%； 產品別為DRAM 20%、NAND 28%、
SIP 9%、 邏輯43%。其中 NAND 出貨仍以 eSSD 為主不管是價跟量都有顯著的提升。
超豐受惠於客戶需求強勁、產品價格調整及簽訂長約(LTA)，帶動營收雙位數成長。晶
兆成(TeraPower)與 TeraPro 因 AI 測試需求暢旺，擴大資本支出滿足客戶，營收展現
高雙位數成長。 
4Q26 受惠於漲價趨勢，2026 年營運大幅改善 
以目前市況來看， MU 仍持續擴大 DRAM 委外訂單 (Mobile、graphics DRAM 、
LPDDR5)，NAND 景氣回溫需求明顯，國票預估力成 4Q26 營收 264.32 億元(QoQ
＋4.33%)，毛利率因價格調整上升到 24.2%，較前一季增加 0.2ppts，Logic 產品也轉
往高毛利為主，淨利為 32.68 億元，QoQ＋25.1%，YoY+75.4%，EPS 4.31 元，在
DRAM/Flash 景氣仍處在高檔的狀況下 ， 子公司超豐也往高附加價值的FCBGA 為主，
Tera Probe 以高階測試為主，高階記憶體/LTA 比重持續上揚，也將帶動力成營收及毛
利率進一步放大，DRAM：訂單能見度高、供不應求，產能利用率可望維持相當水準；
客戶新產能陸續開出為後續觀察點 ，NAND／SSD： 企業級SSD 與 NAND 需求強勁，
第四季整體需求維持高檔；封裝材料成本上升已適度反映於客戶價格，對第四季營收
與毛利率有正面支撐。AI 需求帶動高階 FCBGA 產能需求擴大，第四季高階封裝業務
延續成長；原物料上漲帶動的價格調整已獲客戶接受，預期進一步推升營收與獲利。
投顧預估力成 2026 年營收將達 961.96 億元，EPS 13.10 元。 
2027 年展望持續樂觀 
AI Chip 、OE 產品/CPO/TSV interconnection DRAM 目前驗證進度順利，量產計畫
維持不變，目標成為全球第一家量產 FOPLP AI 應用的公司、與 Broadcom 共同開發
及生產用於先進封裝基板上的加成式細線寬重佈層封裝技術順利， 力成堅持採用  
510mm×515mm 面板尺寸 ， 相較於310mm×310mm 具備更高面積使用率與產出效
益，且技術門檻優於 600mm×600mm。第三季成功量產大尺寸(約 75mm×78mm)
晶片封裝 ， 第四季將進一步跨入更大尺寸(如 78mm×88mm)整合 SoC 與 DRAM 之異
質封裝我們預估力成在整體 FOPLP/記憶體市況良好的狀況下 ，2027 年營收為 1,139.7
億元(YoY+18.4%)，EPS 可以達到 17.97 元優異水準。 
維持買進評等，目標價 306 元 
公司為封測大廠，先進封裝相關業務(Fan-out BGA 產品、Fan-Out POP 、TSV 等)已
佔集團營收三成以上。而目前與 AMD 合資 FOPLP 將於 2027 年小量貢獻營收，記憶
體市場 2027 年整體供需結構不變有利於封測價格的調整，使得力成獲利不再受到原
物料的衝擊，獲利能力大幅度改善，維持正向看法，過去本益比區間位於 6X~20X，
目標價 306 元(2027F PE 17X)。

## Document 175: 6894衛司特 第一金

### Page 1

陳祐竹 
(886-2)2563-1666 
yorick.chen@firstsec.com.tw

衛司特(6894) 
買進, TP 500 (潛在報酬 19.05%)  
買進 
Price(10/22):420 
Target Price：500

衛司特 26H1 獲利翻倍，EPS 達 9.7 元，逼近去年全年水準，主因
AI 伺服器帶動 PCB 高階電鍍製程由可溶性銅球轉向不溶性陽極鈦
網的剛性替代，帶動陽極鈦網營收年增約 50%，加上東南亞新廠
陸續投產使 BOO 業務穩健放量，以及半導體設備銷售與維護大幅
成長。展望後市，隨著 PCB 產業往高階多層板推進、層數與複雜
度提升，而面板廠 「鋁改銅」 趨勢確立，產業規格升級使含銅廢液
處理需求大增， PCB、面板及半導體業客戶積極擴產，加上切入
京東方供應鏈，跨廠複製動能使未來成長具想像空間，BOO 再生
金屬服務 、 陽極鈦網耗材及半導體電解設備與維護三大業務皆具高
成長性，轉投資城鋒科技也將成為新的成長動能。綜合以上，研究
部預估 2026/2027 年 EPS 21/31.28 元，以未來四季 EPS 計算，
給予本益比 20x，目標價 500 元，投資建議為買進。

⚫ 26H1 獲利翻倍 三大業務齊揚推升營運 
衛司特 26Q2 營收 5.91 億元，QoQ+14.7%、YoY+71.5%，
稅後淨利 1.24 億元，QoQ+15.4%、YoY+124.6%，EPS 5.19
元；26H1 營收 11.06 億元，YoY+65.8%，稅後淨利 2.31 億
元，YoY+98.5%，EPS 9.7 元。受惠 AI 伺服器發展引發的高
階電鍍製程材料替代 ，26H1 陽極鈦網營收 YoY 約 50%，PCB
客戶東南亞廠區陸續投產 、 加上高層數高密度蝕刻帶動含銅廢
液量增加，使 26H1 BOO 業務 YoY+30~40%，而 26H1 半導
體設備銷售與維護達 1.07 億元，已接近去年全年 1.12 億元。
高毛利的半導體設備比重提升，加上平均銅價 YoY 約 40%，
帶動 26Q2 毛利率年增 2.95 百分點至 37.07%。

⚫ 規格升級疊加擴廠潮及新廠滲透 多動能推升 2027 年成長 
衛司特 26Q3 營收 6.12 億元，QoQ+3.7%、YoY+74.5%。
RecoCell BOO 業務面向 PCB 及光電面板產業 ， 截至9 月底 ，
共有 67 個 PCB 案場及 11 個光電面板案場 ，PCB 產業方面，
隨著產業往高階多層板推進 ， 層數與複雜度提升使每單位面積
蝕刻出的含銅廢液濃度顯著增加 ， 疊加客戶積極擴增國內產能
與東南亞新廠陸續投產，帶動大量含銅廢水處理需求；此外，
傳統可溶性磷銅球會隨電鍍耗損縮小且易產生汙泥，在 AI 伺

第一金證券投資顧問股份有限公司(First Capital Management Inc.) 台北市長安東路一段 35 號 3F   TEL(02) 2563 -1666 
營業執照字號:114 金管投顧新字第 013 號 
2 
除息日 2026/03/25 服 器 高 深 寬 比 通 孔 電 鍍 要 求 趨 嚴 、 高 速 基 板 材 料 升 級 至
M8/M9 等級驅動下，尺寸安定的不溶性陽極鈦網替代傳統可
溶性磷銅球，已成為不可逆的產業趨勢。光電面板產業方面，
隨著顯示規格升級，帶動面板廠在高品質與高世代產線推動
「鋁改銅」 ，使含銅廢液處理需求大增，今年公司正式打入中
國面板龍頭京東方供應鏈，京東方新產線全面導入銅製程，衛
司特於今年 6 月開始進駐並貢獻月營收 700~800 萬，目前公
司僅服務京東方一個廠區，預計今年底或明年切入第二個廠，
並規劃在未來 2~3 年爭取 7~8 個廠區合作 ， 後市具想像空間 。
BOO 再生金屬服務約 30%與銅價連動，全球 AI 算力與電力
基礎建設的發展使銅價長線具剛性支撐 ， 高檔震盪的銅價環境
有利於衛司特業務發展。綜合以上，研究部預估 2027 年
RecoCell BOO 業務成長 20%， 陽極鈦網可望維持高達約50%
之成長動能。

半導體產業方面，衛司特銷售 RecoCell 電解回收設備與維
護，2025 年半導體營收 1.12 億元，隨台灣半導體大廠積極擴
產 ， 預估2026 年半導體營收成長至 1.8 億元 ，2027 年進一步
躍升至 3 億元，2026~2027 年連續兩年皆維持 60%~70% 的
高年增率。

⚫ 轉投資布局深化循環經濟生態系 
轉投資方面，衛司特今年初以 1.93 億元取得城鋒科技 51%股
權，主要經營廢鉛蓄電池處理與回收，擁有 1000 噸的鉛酸電
池月處理許可，26H1 實際月處理量約 400~600 噸廢電池，
26H1 挹注衛司特營收 9300 萬元；為了提升效益，26H2 城鋒
暫停運轉以執行產能改善計畫，預計於年底將月產能提升至
1000 噸並實現轉虧為盈 ， 後續將進一步導入新設備 ， 待27Q1
設備建置完成，將申請擴充月處理量至 4000~5000 噸，盼
27H2 獲批後開始放量。此外，衛司特董事會於 9 月決議斥資
6 億元 ， 以每股60 元認購天瑛公司現金增資案 ， 取得20.83%
股權，天瑛公司為台灣最大規模的硫酸銅製造商，衛司特將結
合自身的銅廢水回收技術與天瑛的特化品精煉技術 ， 共同打造
高純度硫酸銅的封閉式全循環供應鏈。

綜合以上，研究部預估衛司特 2026 年營收 23.41 億元，
YoY+69.9%， 毛利率37.1%， 稅後淨利5 億元 ，YoY+91.1%，
除權日 - - 
殖利率(%) 2.27

⚫ 結論與建議 
衛司特 26H1 獲利翻倍，EPS 達 9.7 元，逼近去年全年水準，
主因 AI 伺服器帶動 PCB 高階電鍍製程由可溶性銅球轉向不溶
性陽極鈦網的剛性替代，帶動陽極鈦網營收年增約 50%，加
上東南亞新廠陸續投產使 BOO 業務穩健放量，以及半導體設
備銷售與維護大幅成長。展望後市，隨著 PCB 產業往高階多
層板推進 、 層數與複雜度提升 ， 而面板廠 「鋁改銅」 趨勢確立，
產業規格升級使含銅廢液處理需求大增 ，PCB、 面板及半導體
業客戶積極擴產，加上切入京東方供應鏈，跨廠複製動能使未
來成長具想像空間，BOO 再生金屬服務、陽極鈦網耗材及半
導體電解設備與維護三大業務皆具高成長性 ， 轉投資城鋒科技
也將成為新的成長動能。綜合以上，研究部預估 2026/2027
年 EPS 21/31.28 元 ， 以未來四季EPS 計算 ， 給予本益比20x，
目標價 500 元，投資建議為買進。

## Document 190: [000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서_9ee69886

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상SK하이닉스(000660) 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서_9ee69886.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-12

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

SK하이닉스 (000660)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상  
3Q26 영업이익: 상반기와 다르다. 억제 요인들의 추세 반전 전환 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 전분기 대비 32% 증가한 80.1조원으로 시장기대
치를 능가할 전망이다. 최근 원화강세 흐름 및 상반기 동사의 상대적 실적개선 속도 
열위에 3Q26 영업이익 추정치는 70조원까지 내려갔다 (컨센서스 74조원). 하지만 
이제 동사 출하량 및 판가의 탄력적 복원세가 경쟁사 보다 빠른 실적 개선을 이끌 전
망이다. 3Q26 DRAM과 NAND 출하량/판가 증감률은 각각 +13%/+22%, 
+4%/+15% 수준으로, 각 영업이익은 63조원/17조원으로 예상된다 (OPM 각각 
84%/69% 추정). 그간의 상대적 실적 열위는 이제 반전된다. 이는 1) 판가 전략에 
있어 중장기적 관점에서 고객 중심 유연성 극대화를 꾀했던 부분의 보상적 되돌림이 
발생하고, 2) 업계에서 제일 높은 HBM 판매 비중으로 인해 판가 상승률에 발목 잡혔
지만, 오히려 HBM4 본격 공급이 시작되며 이익기여가 본격화된다. 아울러 3) 모바
일 핵심고객과의 기성 계약이 산업 전반적 LTA 논의로 재검토되며 공급사에 크게 유
리한 조건으로 변화되어 가기 시작한다. 당사는 1Q27부터 모바일 메모리 판가 급등
을 전망하며 (그림1), 2026E/2027E 영업이익의 경우 264조원/454조원으로 상향조정한
다. 4Q26 내 시장 실적 추정치 상향조정 및 투자자 수급의 구조적 개선을 전망한다.

‘지능기술’ 시대, 수요의 가격 역탄력성 속 중장기 기회창출에 주목 
과거 B2C 사이클과 다르게, 금번 B2B 사이클은 수요의 가격 역탄력성을 보이고 있
다. AGI 도달을 위한 선착순 투자가 핵심이기 때문이다. 빅테크 중 낙오자가 발생하
기 전까지 경쟁적 대규모 투자는 지속된다. 최근 동사의 솔리다임 상장 추진보도에 
일부 우려가 제기되고 있지만, 투자자들은 그 이면의 중장기적 기회에 주목해야 한다. 
동사는 LTA 외에도, 다양한 파트너십을 추진하며 중장기 시장 예측력을 증가시키고 
있다. 솔리다임 상장 과정에서 유입되는 현금은 모회사 ‘SK AI co’의 지분교환 및 JV 
구축 등에 활용되리라 예상된다. 이는 중장기 수요를 ‘창출’시키는 개념이다.

순수한 주주가치 제고 목적 환원 재도출 예상. 적정주가 상향조정 
빠르게 증가한 현금흐름 중 ‘잉여’분은 주주가치 ‘회복’에 우선 배분돼야 함이 마땅하
며, 동사 역시 최근 공시를 통해 주주가치 제고와 주가의 저평가 인식을 드러냈다. 현
재 진행중인 40조원 자사주 매입 프로그램 종료 후 즉각적인 신규 환원 정책 도출이 
예상된다. 2027년 메모리 공급부족은 명확하다. 역사적으로 효율적 시장 내, 주가 조
정은 탄력적 복원으로 귀결되어왔다. 동사에 대한 투자의견 Buy를 유지하며, 적정주
가의 경우 320만원으로 상향한다 (26E/27E 평균 P/B 4.0배 적용). 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 12

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 3,200,000원 
현재주가 (10.8) 1,681,000원 
상승여력 90.4%

현대차 (005380)

26.3Q Review: 이제 올라가야 할 때 Company Brief

본업 Review: 26.3Q 매출액 45.1조원, 영업이익 2.6조원  
현대차는 26.3Q 매출액 45.1원 (-3.4% YoY, -8.3% QoQ), 영업이익 2.6조원 (+5.5% 
YoY, -6.1% QoQ , +5.9% OPM ) 등의 실적을 시현했다 [iM증권 추정치 매출액: 
45.3조원, 영업이익: 2.4조원]. 매출액은 시장 컨센서스에 부합했고, 영업이익의 경우 
시장 컨센서스였던 2.4조원을 소폭 상회했다. 주요 구성 요소 별 내용은 아래와 같다.  
① P: 26.3Q HEV 판매 대수는 16.6만대를 기록하며 전사 판매 대비 비중 
18.2% 달성 (+18.8% YoY, -11.1% QoQ). 미국의 경우 국내 공장 생산 
감소에 따른 수출 부진으로 도매 판매는 소폭 감소했으나, 판매 비중은 
25.2%로 전반적인 믹스는 직전 분기와 유사. 26.3Q 평균환율이 직전 분기 
대비 크게 하락했으나, 전년 동기 기저 탓에 오히려 + 환율 효과 관찰됨. 
② Q: 26.3Q 도매 판매 대수는 91.8만대로 전년 동기 대비 11.5% 감소 (이하 
YoY 기준, 미국: -10.0%, 유럽: -18.1% 국내: -27.2% 인도: + 18.3%). 
26.2Q 안전 공업 화재에 이어 26.3Q에 발생한 파업의 영향으로 생산 
대수는 감소. 국내 공장에서의 생산 차질은 내수 판매에 더해 수출 감소, 즉 
전체적인 판매 축소로 연결됨. 26.4Q부터 생산 관련 노이즈는 발생하지 
않을 전망. 이에 더해 아반떼, 투싼 등의 신차 판매 효과를 통한 반등 가능.  
③ C: 26.3Q 관세 비용은 직전 분기와 유사한 수준으로 발생한 것으로 파악 
(25.2Q 0.8조원, 25.3Q 1.8조원, 25.4Q 1.5조원, 26.1Q 0.8조원, 26.2Q 
0.9조원). 이란 사태에 따른 주요 원자재 가격 상승 압력은 계속 존재하나, 
내부 원가 절감 정책으로 약 5,000억원의 원가 상승분을 상쇄. 현대차는 
26.4Q 원가 절감 정책의 효과가 더 크게 나타날 것이라고 언급. 26.3Q 
기말 환율이 전년 동기 대비 급락함에 따라 판매보층충당부채전입액 또한 
감소 (26.3Q 매출원가, 판매관리비 각 전년 동기 대비 1.7%, 19.2% 감소). 
금융 부문의 경우 26.3Q 매출액 9.1조원 (+27.8% YoY) , 영업이익 4,710억원 (-
18.2% YoY, -37.9%) 등의 실적을 기록했다. 완성차 판매가 전반적으로 
위축되었음에도 외형 성장을 이어갔으나, 금리 상승에 따른 이자 비용 등 전반적인 
조달 관련 비용이 증가했다. 일부 EV를 중심으로 중고차 가격이 소폭 하락한 점 또한 
HCA 등 주요 부문 이익 감소의 배경이다. 26.3Q 현대차 순이익은 1,932억원을 
기록했는데, 이는 영업외손익이 전년 동기 789억원에서 229억원으로 감소했기 
때문이다. 현대차는 주요 환율 하락에 따른 외화환산손실 증가의 영향이라고 밝혔다. 
1) 생산 단에서의 노이즈가 연속해 발생하며 내수 판매 및 수출 감소가 불가피했으나, 
2) 원자재 가격 상승 영향을 내부 원가 절감 정책을 통해 상쇄했고, 3) 주요 
평균환율이 하락했으나 기말환율 또한 급락하며 판매보증충당부채전입액 감소 효과가 
나타나 시장 기대를 소폭 상회하는 수익성이 관찰되었다는 것이 26.3Q 실적 총평이다. 
현대차는 2026년 도매 판매 가이던스를 416만대에서 400만대로 하향 조정했다. 
26.3Q 누적 도매 판매 대수는 288만대이기에 하향 조정은 불가피했다. 단 신규 
가이던스에 따르면 26.4Q 판매 목표는 약 110만대 수준이라는 것인데, 아반떼, 투싼, 
주요 모델 페이스리프트, GV80 HEV 등의 신차 효과가 얼마나 나타날지가 관건일 
것이다 (이하 분기 도매 판매, 25.1Q 100.1만대, 25.2Q 105.7만대, 25.3Q 103.8만대).  
Buy (Maintain)

미래 사업 업데이트: 자율주행은 계획대로, Atlas는 조금씩 변신중
26.3Q 컨퍼런스 콜에서 특별히 SDV~Boston Dynamics 관련 업데이트는 확인되지
않았으나 여러 매체를 통해 파악된 현대차그룹의 미래 사업 진행 상황은 다음과 같다.
① SDV/자율주행: 데이터 플라이 휠에 대한 중요성 강조. 27년 말부터 광주
권역 자율주행 실증 사업에 투입되는 차량은 Atria AI 활용. SDV 플랫폼이
적용된 차량 양산 시작은 29년. 양산 SDV 출하량 증가함에 따라 29년부터
GPU 5 만개 이상으로 구성되는 새만금 데이터센터를 자체 운영을 목표함.
박민우 포티투닷 사장 겸 AVP 본부장이 부임한 이후 Tesla, nVIDIA 출신의
개발 인력을 충원 중인 상황. 26.05 그랜저 페이스리프트 모델에 플레오스
탑재 시작. 26.09부터 판매되고 있는 신형 아반떼에도 플레오스 탑재 완료.

② Boston Dynamics: 10.8 Rohit Prasad를 CEO로 선임. Rohit Prasad은
아마존에서 Alex 개발에 중추적인 역할을 수행했던 인물. 현대차그룹에서
직접 하드웨어 개발 및 양산을 챙기고, 소프트웨어에 역량을 높이겠다는
행보로 판단. 26.10 Atlas의 신규 덱스트러스를 공개했는데, 기존 제품 대비
자유도가 크게 증가 (7DoF→14DoF). 순수 직구동 방식을 여전히
고수함.10.27 Boston Dynamics가 주요 부품 업체에게 Atlas 1만대 물량의
부품 PO를 곧 발송할 것이라는 기사가 보도됨. 28년까지 연 3만대 CAPA의
공장 완공이라는 기존 계획 대비 양산 및 개발 속도가 빨라진다는 시그널임.
현대차그룹의 미래 사업 업데이트를 또 찾아볼 수 있을 가장 빠른 이벤트는 CES
2027이다. 다만 CES 2027에는 현대차가 아닌 그룹사가 참여함에 따라 해당 행사에서

유의미한 업데이트 내용은 찾아보기 힘들 것이며 이에 중장기 관점의 접근이 필요하다.
시장의 우려가 반영된 주가 하락: 빨라질 속도와 올라올 본업에 주목
현대차 주가는 26.2H 접어들며 크게 하락하고 있고, CES 2026 전후의 상승폭을 사실상
모두 반납했다 (연초 기준 -1.3%, 26.2H 이후 -41.7%). 또한 최근 시장 반등
흐름에서도 사실상 소외되고 있다. 현대차를 바라보는 투자자들의 우려는 아래와 같다.
첫째, CID 전후로 Boston Dynamics 관련 업데이트가 너무 부족하다는 것이다. 26.08
현대차 CID 행사를 앞두고 많은 투자자들은 빅테크 업체들의 지분 투자 및 Atlas
개발과 관련해 새로운 내용을 기대한 것이 사실이다. 이런 상황에서 유럽, 내수 등 주요
지역 에서의 판매 업황 또한 좋지 않다 보니, 시장의 실망은 더욱 컸다. 전술한 것과
같이 CES 2027에 현대차가 참여하지 않는다는 점에서 내년에 대한 기대도 크지 않다.

둘째, 내수와 유럽 업황이 그룹사 대비 너무 좋지 못하다는 점이다. 26.3Q 현대차 내수,
유럽 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 각 27.2%, 18.1% 감소했다. 반면 26.3Q 기아 내수,
유럽 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 각 2.2%, 18.7% 증가했다. 특히 유럽에서의 부진은
EV 라인업이 상대적으로 빈약하고, 중국 업체와의 경쟁이 심화되고 있다는 구조적
문제에서 출발하기에, 투자자들은 26년을 넘어 27, 28년의 성장까지 우려하고 있다
단 우리는 Boston D ynamics의 Atlas는 양산이라는 방향성이 설정된지 오래되지
않았다는 점을 기억해야 한다. 초기 단계부터 양산을 위해 개발된 Tesla Optimus 대비
개발 속도가 빠를 수는 없다. 1~2만대 수준의 데모 제품이 확보되고, 이를 바탕으로
데이터 수집이 일정 부분 확보되는 이후부터 관련 사업 확대 속도는 빨라 질것이다.

취약 지역 판매 업황의 경우에도 내수를 시작으로 점진적으로 개선될 것이다. 생산 차질
외에도 Tesla 및 중국 OEM들의 시장 침투가 우려됨은 분명하지만, 주력 차종 중에서도
국내 판매 볼륨이 큰 아반떼와 투싼의 신차 효과는 분명 기대되는 지점이다. 유럽에서의
반등은 시간이 필요할 것이나, 일단 아이오닉 3가 26.4Q부터 본격적으로 판매될
예정이다. 단 이는 큰 변동 없이 IAA가 2028년부터 시행된다는 전제에 근거하고 있다.
현대차 목표주가 600,000원 유지: 여전한 Multiple 할증 요인
현대차 목표주가를 600,000원으로 유지한다. 26년 EPS 기준 6~8배 수준의 P/E
Multiple을 부여받고 있는 Peer Group들은 중국 시장 M/S 축소로 수익성이 급락하고
있고, 미래 사업 경쟁력은 여전히 전무하다. 현대차의 Multiple 할증 요인은 충분하다.

그림17. 유럽 생산 OEM 생산원가 추이 
 `  
자료: iM증권 리서치본부

IAA는 EU판 IRA로 볼 수 있음. 현대차그룹의 경우 현대차 (터키, 체코)와 기아 (슬로바키아) 모두 유렵 현지
생산 라인을 이미 확보했고, 현대모비스를 중심으로 한 부품 자급률 비중 이미 높음. 또한 주요 부품에 있어
FTA 체결국의 원산지를 EU산과 동일하게 적용하는 규정 존재. 실제 발효까지 큰 변수가 없다면 국내 완성차
업체의 경우 구조적으로 수혜 클 것. 단 중국 업체들에 대한 명시적 배제 규정이 없다는 점은 한계 중 하나. 
IAA 주요 조항 주요 내용
적용 대상
공공조달 차량, 공적 지원(구매 보조금 등)을 받는 차량, 법인차가 대상임. 전기
차, PHEV, 수소차에 적용됨. 2028년부터 회원국은 무공해 및 저공해이면서 EU
산인 차량이 아니면 법인차를 지원할 수 없음. 판매 금지가 아니라 수요 측 혜택
을 역내 생산과 연동하는 구조
역내 부품 요건
1단계(발효 6개월 후)는 EU 조립, 배터리 제외 부품가치 70% EU산, 셀 포함
배터리 핵심부품 3개 EU산을 요구함. 2단계(1단계 3년 후)는 배터리 핵심부품
5개(셀, 양극재,BMS 포함)와 전동 파워트레인·전장 가치 50% EU산이 추가됨.
소형차 특례
전장 4.2m 이하 전기 승용차가 대상임. EU 조립에 더해 부품가치 70%와
배터리 핵심부품 3개 중 하나만 충족하면 됨. 충족 시 CO2 규제상 1.3대로 인정
됨(2025~34년).
FDI 조건 1억 유로 초과 투자가 대상이며, 지분 49% 상한, EU 인력 50% 이상, EU산 투입재 30% 이상 등의 조건이 붙음.
FT A·관세동맹국 예외
FT A나 관세동맹을 맺은 국가의 부품은 EU산과 동등한 부품으로 인정되며,
정부조달협정(GPA) 가입국도 일부 조항에 포함됨. 단 최종 조립국까지 인정되
는지는 불명확하며, 조문상으로는 EU, EEA 역내 조립만 인정되는 것으로 해석
됨. 따라서 한국산 부품은 EU산으로 인정 가능하나, 한국이나 튀르키예에서 조
립한 완성차는 요건 미충족 가능성이 높음.
FT A 예외 배제 조건
집행위는 위임법으로 특정국을 동등 대상에서 제외할 수 있음. 해당국이 EU 기업
에 내국민 대우를 하지 않는 경우, 공급망 의존도나 공급 안보 위협이 있는 경우,
해당 협정의 예외 조항에 해당하는 경우가 배제 사유임. 중국은 FT A가 없어 애초
에 대상이 아님.
2020 2021 2022 2023
대당 생산원가 30,683 32,029 35,079 38,140
대당 판매가격 34,404 27,557 31,244 33,560
1인당 인건비 70,981 74,904 78,348 88,198
통상적으로 중국 OEM의 생산 원가 (중국 생산 가정)은 글로벌 OEM 대비 30% 낮다고 추정됨. BYD의 경우 돌핀 등과 같
은 C 세그먼트 차량에서의 유럽 수익성이 낮지만, A~B 세그먼트 급의 돌핀 서프의 경우 매우 높은 수익성 시현. 이는 유럽
시장 내의 A~B 세그먼트 경쟁 강도가 낮다는 점에서 기인. 즉 100% 수준의 관세가 부과되지 않는다면 BYD의 가격 경쟁
력은 유지되는 것. 따라서 EU는 2,000유로 수준의 중국 OEM의 대당 이익을 상쇄하기 위해 이에 준하는 수준으로 지급되
는 EV 구매 보조금 및 법인 차량 세제혜택을 검토 중인 것이고, 이를 IAA에 담아낼지가 핵심.
유럽 생산 OEM 생산원가 및 판매가격 추이

장 HEV 생산을 통한 시장 진출 속도 빨라질 것으로 전망
인도: GM 탈레가온 공장 인수를 통한 생산량 확대에 나설 예정이고, 이
를 통한 인도 내수 수요 대응 및 글로벌 신흥 시장 진출 또한 목표

통해 EV 수요 회복 국면을 앞두고 있는 유럽 시장 대응에 나설 예정

고 있음. 스포티지 및 셀토스 등 HEV를 통한 기존 차급 대응 강화 전망

당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상 
산업추천 투자등급

 Overweight(비중확대) 
 Neutral(중립) 
 Underweight(비중축소)

## Document 279: Cliff Notes_ managing expectations (2026-11-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ managing expectations (2026-11-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-01
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:04 October 23 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’ attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor
Hauser's fireside chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a follow-up rate hike at the
November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook was "materially worse" than the RBA's

### Page 2

August forecasts. Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having "crystallised" ahead of the
September meeting – the Middle East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser noted the
Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in
August”. He then positioned AI and data centre developments among an "extraordinary sequence of
positive demand shocks around the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through into

CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy Governor noted that "inflation's been coming
in at or a little bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the labour market". He went on to
state that the staff are currently assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement on
capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other words, revise up their assumption for the
NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of market pricing for a follow-up November

hike, from just a 20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition today ahead of next week's
Q3 CPI. For that release, we are forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a 0.9 (3.5%yr)
increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3
as well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast. Provided inflation prints broadly in line
with our expectations, the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.

Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3 and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to
3.7%yr. Under the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely watched measures of core
inflation took a step higher to now be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our NZ
economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October
policy review, while retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.

Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised to the upside in Q3, GDP growth accelerating from
0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on track to
achieve their 4.5-5.0% full-year growth objective.
While annual retail sales growth improved in September, rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace
of growth is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very weak versus history. Consumer
spending continues to be constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to employment

prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five years into the property downturn, house prices continue
to decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep downturn, -20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in
high-tech investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst domestically oriented businesses
as well as property’s malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and 2028 as the

contribution from net exports moderates, broad-based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect
recent targeted support measures to be broadened and given much greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr in September, exceeding both market
expectations and the Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely driven by energy, fuel
prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services

inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an immediate concern inflation, the labour market
showing resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to 4.7% in August, suggests demand

### Page 3

Related articles
risks for inflation will need close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is
released. For both events, the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The week after, the
US mid-term elections are due.

Weekly Economics
U.S. Week Ahead
Existing Home Sales • Thursday
Housing market conditions remain highly unfavorable. Mortgage
rates are the primary headwind, and they have climbed even higher
alongside the rise in longer-term yields. At the same time, low
supply is preventing a broad correction in home prices, leaving
prospective buyers squeezed on two fronts.
We expect that existing home sales slid 3.4% in August to an
annualized rate of 3.92 million. Behind this forecast is ongoing

weakness in mortgage applications and a recent bounce in
mortgage rates, which rose from 6.43% at the beginning of July to
6.66% by month-end.
We do not expect affordability conditions to meaningfully improve
in the near term. Supply is likely to remain constrained while high
mortgage rates discourage mobility. And mortgage rates are apt
to remain elevated amid firm economic growth expectations and
heavy government and corporate debt issuance.

### Page 3

Economics Week Ahead Economics
CPI • Friday
Headline inflation looks to have picked up last month. We estimate
the CPI rose 0.40% in August, with gasoline prices increasing a little
over 4% as renewed tensions in the Middle East pushed oil higher.
Grocery prices also likely rebounded after modestly declining in July.
Outside of food and energy, price pressures appear little changed.
We expect the core CPI to rise 0.23%, essentially matching July's

pace. Core goods inflation should remain firm, led by another solid
increase in used vehicle prices as consumer prices continue to
catch up to the trend indicated at wholesale auctions. Meantime,
household furnishings and other tariff-sensitive categories appear
to be stabilizing after declining earlier this year.
Core services inflation should be little changed with another 0.2%
monthly increase. But underneath the surface, we expect a modest
pickup in travel (led by lodging away from home) to offset slightly

cooler medical care and housing cost growth.
All told, we expect the report to show that while headline inflation
remains influenced by the ebbs and flows of the conflict in the
Middle East, core inflation remains contained. Should our forecast
prove correct, core CPI will ease to 2.4% year-over-year, in line with
its 6-month annualized rate but above the more recent 3-month
annualized pace of 1.7%.

### Page 4

Weekly Economics
G10 Week Ahead
European Central Bank Monetary Policy Meeting •
Thursday
We expect the European Central Bank (ECB) to raise the Deposit
Rate by 25 bps to 2.50% next week. August headline inflation
remained elevated at 3.3% year-over-year, though softer core
and services inflation suggest underlying pressures continue to
moderate. Still, the more hawkish tone of the July minutes and
renewed rise in energy prices should keep policymakers cautious.

The recent escalation in the Middle East has pushed fuel prices
at the pump close to their 2022 peaks, while natural gas storage
remains below historical levels, leaving the Eurozone vulnerable
to further energy price increases. The ECB is unlikely to respond
aggressively if the shock remains confined to energy given the
downside risks to growth. However, a broader rise in food inflation
linked to El Niño would be more concerning, as highly visible prices

could lift inflation expectations and generate second-round effects.
For now, resilient Q3 activity, elevated headline inflation and
contained wage growth support one final hike. The ECB will likely
retain and emphasize its scenario-based outlook in the updated
staff projections given continued uncertainty around the conflict
and energy prices. The policy path beyond September will depend
on whether energy and food pressures persist and begin to spread,

which could leave room for further tightening.

U.K. Monthly GDP • Friday
We expect UK GDP to rise 0.2% month-over-month in July, with
growth of 0.3% on a three-month-over-three-month basis.

Activity likely moderated after June’s strong rebound, though
services remained supportive as the July services PMI rose to 52.1.
Retail sales volumes fell 0.5% month-over-month, while softer
manufacturing activity likely reflected some reversal of earlier
precautionary stockbuilding.
The moderation appears more consistent with payback from
June than a broader deterioration in momentum. August PMIs
remain consistent with Q3 GDP growth of around 0.3% quarter-

over-quarter. Still, the renewed escalation in the Middle East and
associated rise in gas and energy prices adds upside risk to inflation
and could weigh on household demand. Against this backdrop and
with Bank of England hawks continuing to emphasize the need for
tighter policy, we expect the BoE to remain cautious and continue
to look for a rate hike in Q4. -1.0%

### Page 5

Economics Week Ahead Economics
EM Week Ahead
Mexico CPI • Wednesday
We expect Mexico’s August inflation rate to edge higher, with
headline CPI rising to 3.34% year-over-year (0.27% month-over-
month) from 3.26% year-over-year in early August. Part of the
pickup likely reflects less favorable base effects and a smaller
disinflationary contribution from food prices. While agricultural
prices continued to help restrain inflation in mid-August, the drag

was notably less pronounced than in July. Core inflation should
remain elevated at 3.98% year-over-year (0.25% month-over-
month).
While inflation remains within Banxico’s midpoint 3% target,
persistent core and services inflation suggest convergence toward
the midpoint of the target could prove gradual. For now, we
continue to expect Banxico to keep rates on hold, with the policy
rate remaining at 6.50% through the rest of 2026 and into early
2027 as policymakers await clearer evidence that underlying

inflation pressures are easing sustainably. While rate cuts remain
a possibility in 2027, the current inflation backdrop argues for
patience in the near term.

Brazil CPI • Friday
We expect Brazil’s August inflation rate to slow to 4.20% year-over-
year, helped by lower electricity, food and fuel prices. The main
driver should be a temporary reduction in residential electricity bills
stemming from a one-off credit, while recent data already point to
a notable easing in price pressures. Mid-August CPI came in weaker
than expected, with inflation falling to 4.24% year-over-year amid
declines in electricity, food, fuel and airfare prices. Beyond these

temporary factors, restrictive monetary policy continues to weigh
on economic activity, as evidenced by the recent slowdown in Q2
GDP growth. However, underlying inflation risks remain. A still-
tight labor market and government measures aimed at supporting
consumption could keep services inflation sticky, making progress
toward the 3.0% inflation target more gradual.
Overall, the inflation outlook remains consistent with additional
policy easing. We expect COPOM to deliver a 25 bps rate cut in

September, bringing the Selic Rate to 13.75%. Beyond September,
policymakers are likely to proceed cautiously as they assess
incoming inflation data and the impact of the election cycle on
inflation expectations. Our base case remains for a pause after
September, with additional easing more likely in 2027.

## Document 304: European resilience, American vigour (2026-10-30)

Source PDF: all report pdf/European resilience, American vigour (2026-10-30).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 30 October 2026

EcoFlash

European resilience, American vigour

Eurozone growth continues to prove resilient, notwithstanding the volatility of the Irish figures. The Q3 data reinforce 
our forecast of 1.1% growth this year, which could even be slightly exceeded, as German and Spanish momentum shows 
no sign of fading. In France, the negative surprise (-0.1% q/q against a consensus of +0.1% q/q) stems from a new one-
off factor (the sharp fall in aerospace output caused by delays in input deliveries) and masks an encouraging rebound 
in household consumption. In the United  States, growth reached its highest level since Q2 2025 (0.8% q/q), driven by 
buoyant private domestic demand.

Eurozone: The resilience of growth is confirmed 
Eurozone GDP grew by 0.4% q/q in Q3  according to Eurostat's preliminary flash estimate, above both the consensus and our 
Nowcast (0.3%). The ever -volatile Irish figures contributed 0.2 pp to this outcome. Excluding Ireland, growth (0.2% q/q) 
nonetheless remains in line with the pace of recent quarters (0.2 -0.3% q/q on average). This illustrates the Eurozone's capacity 
to withstand the current shocks, thanks in particular to the strength of Spain (+0.07 pp contribution to the q/q change in Eurozone’s 
GDP) and the Netherlands (+0.04 pp), and to Germany's gradual recovery (+0.06 pp). France (-0.03 pp) and Italy (zero growth), 
by contrast, posted disappointing results, although in France's case these partly reflect temporary factors (aerospace output). The 
growth carry-over for 2026 now stands at 1.0%, which supports our forecast of 1.1% in annual average terms and even points to 
upside risk. The European Commission's October surveys remain upbeat, with a further improvement in industrial activity 
expectations. 
The German recovery continues . As anticipated by our  Nowcast, real GDP grew by 0.2% q/q in Q3 (after 0.3% in Q2).  
According to Destatis, consumption (private and public) increased slightly, while investment edged down. Export momentum 
slowed markedly compared with Q2. This is Germany's fourth consecutive quarter of growth. 
Italian GDP surprised on the downside, stagnating in Q3 (against a consensus of 0.1%).  According to Istat, this reflects a 
decline in value added in industry, offset by increases in services and agriculture. The positive contribution from domestic demand 
was reportedly cancelled out by a negative contribution from net trade. 
Spanish growth remained robust in Q3, in line with expectations (0.6% q/q, after 0.7% in Q2),  driven mainly by domestic 
demand (0.5 pp contribution) and, to a lesser extent, by net exports (0.1 pp). Consumption remains the main engine of growth 
(+0.6% q/q for private consumption and +0.9% for public consumption). Investment rose by 0.7%. Export gr owth (1.3%) once 
again outpaced import growth (1.1%) this quarter. All sectors except construction recorded positive changes in value added, with 
industry and services both up 0.7%. 
France: A sharp drop in aerospace output prevents a return to growth in Q3 
GDP contracted by 0.1% q/q in Q3 (after stagnating in Q2), weighed down by a sharp fall in aerospace output (-4.5% q/q 
in the production of other transport equipment, which includes aerospace). This steep decline dragged down goods production 
(down 1.2% q/q) and explains the sharp contraction in exports ( -1.7% q/q) and business investment (-0.4% q/q). Net trade thus 
subtracted 0.6 pp from GDP (imports were flat over the same period). Conversely, household consumption surprised on the 
upside (+0.4% q/q), boosted by purchases of electric vehicles (registrations up 112% y/y in Q3). Public consumption 
(+0.3% q/q) also supported growth, as did activity in market services (+0.3% q/q).  Despite the sharp drop in aerospace 
output in Q3, Airbus has confirmed its delivery targets for the year (with engine supply problems expected to be resolved), pointing 
to a strong rebound in growth in Q4 (0.3% q/q according to our forecasts). Overall, gr owth is expected to reach 0.3% in 2026 , 
before picking up to 0.8% in 2027. 
United States: Growth at a one-year high  
GDP growth picked up in Q3 to 3.4% at a seasonally adjusted annualised rate ( AR; i.e. 0.8% q/q), from 2.2% in Q2 
(0.6% q/q). This is the fastest pace since Q3 2025, confirming that activity is almost immune to the energy shock. Private domestic 
demand remains particularly strong. Private non-residential investment accelerated to 12.7% AR, a performance unmatched since 
the post-Covid reopening. AI-related components are driving this figure (18.5% AR), but other types of investment are also rising 
for a second consecutive quarter (3.8% AR) after contracting uninterruptedly throughout 2025. Household consumption, steady 
at 2.5% AR in real terms, is growing faster than it did before inflation picked up again, but at the cost of a significant drop in the 
saving rate (3.8% at the end of the quarter, down from 4.4% at the end of the previous quarter). The United States nonetheles s 
continues to "import its growth": net trade is weighing on GDP owing to a surge in imports (19.7% AR), driven by AI -related 
components – IT, semiconductors and telecommunications (103.8% AR).

### Page 2

Head of Advanced Economies Team, France  
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

Fed Chair Kevin Warsh acknowledged that progress is being
made, but inﬂation remains too high and a December FOMC

James Knightley, Padhraic Garvey, CFA and Chris Turner
Federal Reserve Chair Warsh announced that interest rates remain unchanged
10-2 vote to keep policy unchanged
The Federal Reserve voted 10-2 in favour of leaving the Fed funds
target rate range at 3.75%-4%. Only 2bp of a potential 25bp hike
was priced ahead of the decision and only 6 of 97 organisations
polled by Bloomberg predicted a hike, so this was no surprise and
market reaction has been limited.
Senior Fed oﬃcials, including Vice Chairs Philip Jeﬀerson and
Michelle Bowman and Governor Chris Waller, suggested ahead of
the decision that they were prepared to move cautiously, seeing
little need for back-to-back rate hikes after September's policy
change. Nonetheless, Beth Hammack and Lisa Cook both felt that
the current situation warranted additional action. Cook’s vote for a

There is the valid question that, given a majority of FOMC members
project a rate hike before the end of the year, why didn’t they take
that decision today? Kevin Warsh’s response was that he himself
didn’t make a prediction and, in any case, they removed some
policy accommodation in September. Inﬂation is “going the right
way” and he is encouraged by the progress made.
We will have two jobs reports and one inﬂation print ahead of the
December decision. The November CPI will be released the day after

the Fed meeting, and oﬃcials will almost certainly get to see it. As
such, they will have more information than the market does,
meaning a surprise can’t be completely ruled out. For now, a
December hike looks a solid bet, which would leave the Fed funds
target rate range at 4.00%-4.25%.
The sharp move higher in Treasury yields and the ﬁrmer dollar are
tightening ﬁnancial conditions to a degree, but our forecast path
for the Fed assumes that in the couple of months following the

midterm elections we see an agreement that improves the ﬂow of
energy from the Persian Gulf, but signiﬁcantly, allows for a sharp
reduction in shipping and insurance costs. It's the latter point that is
crucial to delivering lower pump prices at a time when reﬁneries are
working at capacity.
If correct, this should meaningfully ease inﬂation pressures, and
with US wage growth (the biggest cost input for corporate America)
looking very benign at just 3% and the largest component of the

inﬂation basket – housing at 35% by weight – set to experience
more disinﬂation, we believe overall US inﬂation can fall back to 2%
in the second half of 2027.
Hence, we subsequently anticipate a prolonged pause through to
December 2027, when we have that December 2026 hike being
reversed. This is in line with the current consensus forecast, while
the Fed’s own projections (its central forecast) see one hike this year
with rates left unchanged throughout 2027.

break back above 5% for the 10yr

Hardly a word in the short statement that is not leaning hawkish.
But no hike. Prior to the FOMC meeting, it was remarkable how the
10yr yield had been clinging to the 4.99% level. It was like a
comfort level; technically below 5%, but not too far below in case it
needs to turn tail and shoot back up again. It seemed that the US
10yr was waiting to tick oﬀ this outcome before deviating too
much from 5%. Well, it’s now had the outcome. The initial reaction

was up. But the reaction to Chair Warsh’s words at the press
conference was to revert down to 5%, and brieﬂy below. This can be
rationalised by Warsh’s comforting words on inﬂation expectations,
which he deemed to have been tamed somewhat.
That all being said, our interpretation is that this Fed still has a job
to do if the type of price stability that Chair Warsh has described is
to be achieved. He has described price stability as strictly 2%, and

he seems to be serious about hitting that. This is good from a
protective sense for the back end. At the same time, it’s tough to
view the current set-up of still elevated printed inﬂation and a Fed
on hold as all that protective. Also, inﬂation expectations in fact
rose post the Fed decision and subsequent Warsh commentary. We
glean from this enough to validate the move back above 5% on the
10yr. We maintain our view that we’re on a journey back up in the

direction of 5.25%.
The implementation note was broadly unchanged in terms of
action. The various implementation rates are unchanged, as are the
implementation mechanisms. And the Fed will continue to reinvest
principal payments from its MBS securities into Treasury bills. We’re
likely still some way from the Fed spelling out a plan to shrink its
balance sheet, which we know remains a policy objective for Chair
Warsh. We expect recommendations from the various committees

to surface closer to the turn of the year. Meanwhile, liquidity
conditions are stable, with repo behaving far better than it did a
year ago.

## Document 315: France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08)

Source PDF: all report pdf/France_ double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and stable non-performing loans ratio (2026-10-08).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Paris, 8 October 2026

EcoFlash

France: double-digit growth in business creations, a slowdown in the rise in bankruptcies and 
stable non-performing loans ratio

Business creations rose by 12% y/y over the first eight months of 2026 1. This is significantly higher than the 5.3% y/y 
growth recorded in 2025. This increase was driven by sectors linked to information and communication technologies.

Meanwhile, business bankruptcies rose by 5.3% y/y between January and July, according to data from the Banque de 
France (compared with the 3.6% y/y increase for the whole of 2025). The rise was more pronounced in the 1st quarter (+8% y/y) 
before slowing down. This slowdown could persist, as, according to Altares, payment delays have fallen to an average of 12.3 
days in the second quarter of 2026, compared with 14.1 days a year earlier (and 13.1 days for the European average).

Rising bankruptcies are more heavily affecting the services sector, which is also experiencing slightly stronger growth 
in business creations. This correlation is particularly noticeable in the retail sector, as business creations have risen by 13% 
since the start of 2026, while bankruptcies are up by 3%. In construction and manufacturing, business bankruptcies have been 
falling since the second quarter of 2026.

The average ratio of non -performing corporate loans has remained stable at a moderately high level for the past year  
(4.0% of total loans and advances in Q2 2026). In construction, where activity is more cyclical than in other sectors, the ra tio of 
non-performing loans has reached its highest level since 2019, standing at 9.5%.

The proportion of loans that have deteriorated but are not yet non-performing (“Stage 2” according to IFRS 9 terminology) 
is stable, standing at 15.4% q/q, and remains below its all -time high (16.1%). This indicator, which helps to anticipate trends in 
non-performing loans, is relatively encouraging.

Despite a significant increase since 2021, the economic weight of bankruptcies remains, on average, below its peak in 
Q1 2025 (0.53 compared with 0.61, respectively). This indicator generally allows bankruptcies to be weighted based on the size 
of the businesses. However, the economic weight of bankrupted small businesses is at a historically high level (2.11).

Take a look at our EcoCharts on trends in business creations and bankruptcies in France and the 
associated risks. It will be updated on a quarterly basis.

Thomas Humblot 
Economiste, team: Banking economics 
thomas.humblot@bnpparibas.com

Stéphane Colliac 
Head of Advanced economies, France 
stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

1 For more details, see Business creations and bankruptcies in France: double-digit growth in business creations, lower growth in bankruptcies, 
and stable non-performing loans, 8 October 2026.

### Page 3

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
Markets have had little respite over the past month. Hopes 
of a lasting US–Iran agreement have faded and intermittent 
attacks on shipping have kept Brent oil prices in triple digits. 
Workarounds have allowed a meaningful share of crude to 
continue flowing but these arrangements are slower and 
costlier, while depleted inventories leave little room to 
absorb another shock and a range of disruptions to refining 
and shipping are also impacting downstream energy costs. 
Bond markets have been just as restless, with continued lifts 
in government bond yields across much of the globe and 
more sharply in the US. 
Notably, oil is no longer doing the heavy lifting for yields. 
The rise has been concentrated in real yields, with the Fed’s 
September hike – and its signal of more to come – acting as 
something of a trigger. In the US, fiscal concerns have been 
more of a sub-plot, though the Treasury’s efforts to hold 
down long yields have arguably only drawn more attention 
to them. 
In our view, markets are getting ahead of themselves on 
monetary policy offshore. We expect just one more hike 
from the Fed rather than the three the market currently has 
priced, with stretched housing affordability posing risks to 
both consumption and inflation. Central banks in Europe, the 
UK and Canada are also likely to bide their time after a final 
move. Europe is weathering the energy shock better than 
feared and has far more fiscal room to respond than the US – 
one reason we still see the US dollar trending lower over the 
next two years.
The RBA hiked again in September and looks set to follow 
up in November, taking the cash rate to a new cycle high. 
Some of the risks the Board flagged have crystallised, 
but the minutes also reveal a tendency to reach for the 
more hawkish read of the data, in addition to the familiar 
downbeat take on supply capacity. Our base case assumes 
no return to full-scale conflict, but with energy buffers 
depleted, bond markets less forgiving and the RBA already 
leaning hawkish, the margin for error – for markets and 
policymakers alike – is starting to ‘wear thin’.
Australia: After a soft but better than expected first half, the 
Australian economy is facing into more challenges as another 
round of disruptions from global energy markets combine with 
further interest rate rises and a deepening housing downturn. 
The demand mix should see capacity pressures continue to 
ease but with significant risks on either side.
Middle East: Low inventories and higher shipping costs will 
keep oil and fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast 
to average US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by 
Q4 2027 as supply recovers. Risks are two-sided: escalation 
could push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran 
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply.
Commodities: Most commodities rose in September, with 
many of the themes that drove moves across August remaining 
in place. Japanese LNG and Brent oil recorded strong gains 
amid Middle East tensions. Copper and aluminium extended 
recent gains while iron ore was supported by higher freight 
and production costs. Gold fell after the Fed’s September rate 
hike. 
Global FX Markets: In late September and early-October 
the DXY index popped higher to near 103, a high back to 
early-2025. Underlying the move are expectations that 
inflation will prove persistent globally as well as a belief that 
the US’ tech sector leaves it best positioned to weather the 
storm.
New Zealand: Higher fuel costs and a weaker exchange rate 
have lifted the short-term inflation outlook while the economy 
has continued to grow through the Iran war. Growth looks set 
to continue to be buoyed by strong commodity prices and a 
supportive exchange rate. However, downside risks are growing 
from higher energy costs and long-term interest rates. 
United States: The US housing market remains under 
considerable pressure. Prices continue to edge up, supporting 
wealth. But up-and-coming main street consumers are 
struggling with affordability and availability, posing enduring 
risks for both consumption growth and inflation.   
China: With each month that passes, the risk of disappointing 
authorities’ 4.5-5.0% ambition grows. While the Government 
is right to take a targeted approach to support, it must quickly 
multiply its scale if downside risks are to be kept at bay. 
Asia: For much of the past twenty years, Asia has been a 
source of disinflation for the world. But a combination of ageing 
populations, rising labour costs, increasing electricity demand 
and technology sector developments imply Asia may provide 
less support to global disinflation over the decade ahead.
Europe & UK: The euro has come under considerable pressure 
during the past month. That said, the European economy 
continues to tick along nicely while inflation risks remain 
manageable, allowing the ECB to be measured in their response.

Wearing thin

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
The main development affecting global markets this month
has been the sharp lift in selected bond yields, especially in
the US and France. Since the previous Market Outlook was
published, 10-year benchmarks have risen 27bps for US

Treasuries, to be well above the 5% threshold, and 40bps for
French bonds. The latter move contrasts with German bunds,
which have seen little change in yields at that tenor over the
month, though they are up more than 60bps year to date.
Several factors are behind the latest rise, with no single
cause dominating. For some of these factors, the underlying
conditions had been in evidence for some time, but recent
events focused attention on them. The rise has been primarily

a story of real yields: longer-run inflation expectations implied
by both breakevens and swaps have not increased that much.
For much of the year, fluctuations in oil prices – and thus the
near-term inflation outlook – fed into market views of central
bank policy, and longer yields as well. More recently, though,
this correlation has broken down. Shifts in expectations about
central bank policy have remained important for bond pricing,
as evident in the concentration of the increase in real yields.

The FOMC’s decision to increase the Fed funds rate, and flag in
the ‘dot plots’ that at least one more hike was likely, seems to
have been something of a trigger event for repricing, not only
for US yields. Also salient were the various actions taken by
the US Treasury to suppress long yields and improve the US
government’s debt-servicing position. These efforts had little
lasting effect and yields are now higher, and higher relative to
peers, than before. But they may have served to draw attention

to the growing issues around US fiscal sustainability. High
supply of debt from hyperscaler firms to fund the AI / data
centre boom has also likely pressured yields at the margin.
That said, fiscal concerns are unlikely to have been the main
issue driving the lift in US yields. The slope of the US yield
curve is noticeably flatter than at the beginning of the year;
in other words, short yields have risen even more than the
10-year yield, signifying tighter expected monetary policy

lifting yields across the curve. Another lens on this shift is that
2-year and other short-dated yields have increased more than
estimates of risk-neutral rate, such as that from the NY Fed.
Fiscal concerns have been more salient in the case of France,
where spreads to bunds have widened from around 70bps at
the beginning of the year to nearly 140bps currently, wider than
Greece or Italy, with about 40bps of the widening occurring in
the past month. Contagion to other euro area sovereigns has

been limited, with spreads widening around 22bps over the
month for Greece, Italy and Belgium, and 13bps for Spain.
Many factors lifting global bond yields ...
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
... and RBA policy rates outlook
In Australia, the RBA hiked the cash rate again at its late
September meeting, and a further hike at the November
meeting looks likely. This would take the cash rate to 4.85%, a
new cycle high.
While the forecasts the RBA published in August did not

support further rate hikes, in September these forecasts were
characterised as resting on several things going right and
certain risks not emerging. Some of these risks, including a re-
escalation of the war in the Middle East and thus energy prices,
have since crystallised. The post-meeting communication
and minutes also pointed to the July CPI result being above
expectations, as was June quarter GDP growth “at the margin”.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction

costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data
centre boom. No weight is put on the possibility that this new
investment and technology might add to productivity or the
capital stock, and so supply capacity, even with a lag. Indeed,
the post-meeting minutes highlighted the financial stability
risks if valuations in this area should disappoint “even if the
technology were ultimately successful”, as if the latter was not
yet clear. This language points to a generally downbeat view of

Australian supply capacity that also emerges in its discussion
of productivity trends. Although its position piece in the August
2025 Statement on Monetary Policy  indicated that lower trend
growth in productivity had no implications for inflation or
monetary policy, more recent statements imply that it does.
More broadly, the minutes for the September meeting revealed
a tendency to contemplate more hawkish interpretations
of data and outcomes. For example, the minutes dismissed

evidence of the labour market easing a bit more than
was forecast by pointing to the changes to the survey
arrangements. At the same time, the minutes contemplated
that the rate of unemployment consistent with inflation
being at target might have risen, even though wages growth
has marginally undershot the RBA’s forecasts and the cost
pressures cited by its business liaison contacts are mostly
non-labour costs. The minutes also included an extended

discussion of the possibility that the global neutral interest
rate might have increased recently. If true, this would imply
that financial conditions were less restrictive. This conclusion
would be hard to square with yield curves that have flattened
since the beginning of the year and other central bank
estimates of the term premium having increased, both of which
suggest tighter financial conditions.
That said, the RBA will find it hard to look past inflation

outcomes in the second half of year that is likely to remain
sticky. We anticipate trimmed mean inflation to record
quarterly outcomes of around 0.9%qtr in Q3 and Q4, remaining
around the current 3½%yr pulse. The Monetary Policy Board
will want to see clear evidence of inflation coming down before
withdrawing policy restrictiveness, so we continue to pencil in
August as the likely date for start of the rate-cutting phase.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
After posting a soft but better than expected first half, the
Australian economy is facing into more challenges near term
as another round of disruptions from global energy markets
combine with a further rise in interest rates and a deepening

housing downturn. Against this, business investment is in
the midst of a sustained ramp-up led by a large and growing
pipeline of data centre and energy investment projects.
Elsewhere, public demand is growing at a slower pace and
new dwelling investment is likely to moderate but both are
expected to still see positive growth. Overall, the demand
mix should see capacity pressures continue to ease but with
significant risks on either side.
Shifting political winds

Some of these risks are now in the wider policy space. The
political landscape has changed materially since the 2025
Federal Election with opinion polls showing a sustained swing
against the Labor government and a marked rise in support
for One Nation that raises the prospect of a three-horse race
in the next election, the outcome of which could be very hard
to predict (we may get a preview of this with next month’s
Victorian state election). While the next Federal election is

still a long way off – not due until well into 2028 – the shift
does appear to be having some bearing on policy. For example,
the government’s choice not to reinstate the temporary
halving in fuel excise tax that was deployed when fuel prices
first spiked back in March suggests it is less inclined towards
providing broad based cost-of-living support that might make
it harder to bring inflation back down.
Migration policy is shaping more clearly as a battleground.

A suite of changes announced by the government in mid-
September and which came into effect in October, are already
expected to slow working holidaymaker and foreign student
arrivals, moderating total net overseas migration from
around 300k current to 225k by 2028, trimming population
growth from around 1.4%yr to 1.2%yr. For its part, the main
Liberal-National opposition party has proposed to reduce net
migration to 100k in the first two years of government, before

increasing to 130k and 160k in the two years following –
which would likely see total population growth slow to around
0.7-0.8%yr initially. One Nation has proposed a period of ‘net
negative’ migration, lowering net migration by 250k a year
over the first three years of government before considering a
‘ceiling’ of 130k per year thereafter. This would see population
growth slow to around 0.2-0.3%yr initially.
While none of these policies are on the table for this year

or next, the direction of travel is clear with a risk that the
current government moves further towards curbing migrant
More challenges near term ...
Matthew Hassan, Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting
inflows. Slower population growth basically maps one-to-
one to GDP growth although the specific flow-on impacts to
areas like housing and labour markets are more complex and
will depend on the population mix and how the demand and
supply effects balance out. For the record, we have trimmed

our own population growth forecasts in the 2028 out-year,
from 1.4%yr to 1.2%yr with a related tweak to our GDP
growth forecast for that year.
Consumer sentiment-spending gap
Near term challenges continue to centre on the consumer.
Here, the latest updates show sentiment again plumbing
historical lows but more mixed messages around spending
which is still holding up better than might be expected based
on a simple read on the consumer mood.
Certainly that mood remains deeply negative. The Westpac–

Melbourne Institute Consumer Sentiment Index dropped
4.7% to 80.4 in October, putting the month amongst the forty
worst reads since the began in the early 1970s, following
two others reads in this group back in April and June and
another eight recorded in 2022–23. Altogether this marks the
worst period of recurring extremely weak sentiment since the
disastrous recession in the early 1990s.
This has been difficult to square with the soft rather than

outright weak picture on consumer spending through most
of this year. Even when real spending was recording more
material per capita declines back in 2023-24, these were
somewhat milder than what might have been expected given
the weakness in sentiment at the time.
We explored this more fully in our August Red Book . Some
of the disconnect may be a by-product of high inflation which
‘hits different’ sentiment-wise. Cost of living pressures,

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN ECONOMY
... centred on uneven consumer, housing downturn ...
which are the central issue for consumers at the moment,
represent a broad-based but shallower squeeze rather than
the deeper shocks that typically come when big economic
downturns hit. Labour markets appear to be an important

point of distinction – despite the weakness in sentiment,
consumers have been relatively unperturbed about the jobs
outlook in recent years. Adding consumers’ unemployment
expectations to sentiment measures significantly improves its
‘fit’ as a predictor of per capita spending.
There other aspect that helps to reconcile the sentiment-
spending gap is the detailed picture across segments. There
may be one economy but there is not a single consumer.

Instead both sentiment and spending outcomes reflect the
average across a range of often quite varied conditions in
different consumer segments.
This shows through in the dispersion of sentiment across
sub-groups. The chart right shows the current distribution of
index reads across the 100-odd sub-groups we regularly track
with some selected groups called out. If the range seems wide
its because it is – measures of spread over the last two years
have been 50-60% wider than the average over the previous

three decades. Exactly why is not completely clear but the
observations point to a more fractured consumer with multiple
fault lines – including across states, metro/regional areas, voter
groups, labour force status, income levels and housing tenures.
Spending-wise, one of the clearest emerging fault lines is across
age groups. Here, there look to be three main ‘blocs’ – 18-34 year
olds (mainly millennials’); 35-54 year olds (mainly ‘gen X’); and

those aged over 55 (dominated by the ‘baby boomers’).
Demographically, the 55+ age group is both the largest and
the fastest-growing – our estimates suggest it now makes up
about 45% of all households, up 8.5pts compared to 2020. It
is also seeing significant financial shifts with the cohort coming
through carrying materially larger superannuation holdings
(reflecting a variety of factors including a longer period of
compulsory contributions that have earned compounding

returns) and more housing wealth. Again, our rough estimates
suggest average household wealth in this group is around
$2.5m up over $800k since 2020 (reflecting both the
aforementioned demographic shifts and valuation changes).
At the same time, the group has low debt levels – it is largely
untouched by interest rate increases and probably benefits
slightly insofar as this lifts interest income both directly and
indirectly via super holdings.
For 18-34 year olds, incomes are more important than balance

sheets, meaning inflation poses more of a constraint. For 35-
54 year olds, income is less of a constraint but high debt levels
poses a big challenge as rate rises increase servicing costs.
All of which is to say that it should come as little surprise that
spending by the 55+ age group has been running stronger
than the other age groups. Breakdowns from our Westpac
Consumer Panel show average non fuel spend has been rising
at a 5% annual pace over the last six months amongst those
